2250LIFE HACK: HOW TO KNOW WHEN THE SYRUP FOR ZEPHYR IS COOKED WITHOUT A THERMOMETER
2251 
2252#marusya_theory_tips
2253 
2254Experienced zephyr makers know how important it is to start adding boiling syrup to the whipped berry puree in time — if the syrup is undercooked, the zephyr mass will not be thick enough, and if it’s overcooked, the zephyr will quickly crystallize, and when tasting, the sugar will crunch on your teeth.
2255 
2256How to determine that very moment when the syrup is cooked?
2257 
2258There’s a magic number — 110 °C. It is up to this temperature that it is recommended to cook syrup for zephyr. But I’d say that this number is literally “magic” — it’s very difficult to correctly measure the temperature of a boiling syrup:
2259 
2260▫️ firstly, the syrup actively boils, which makes it impossible to correctly measure the temperature on the surface of the syrup using a pyrometer
2261 
2262▫️ secondly, there should be a certain minimum amount of the liquid in the saucepan so that the probe thermometer can correctly read the temperature, and if you don’t make zephyrs in large batches, a small amount of syrup, which is usually required to make them, won’t allow you to do that
2263 
2264Therefore, I never determine the doneness of the syrup by temperature — only by consistency! But how to do that?
2265 
2266💡 Here’s a life hack for you!
2267 
2268Take a small amount of syrup with a spatula and raise it to eye level: if the syrup flows down in a thin stream, then the syrup is ready.
2269 
2270📎 In the carousel, you can find a short video from the live broadcast on the preparation of zephyr, where I clearly show how to check the doneness of syrup visually.
2271WHY ONE SHOULDN’T OPEN THE OVEN WHILE BAKING A SPONGE
2272 
2273#marusya_theory_tips
2274 
2275If you open the oven while baking the sponge, the sponge will deflate, it’s true. But why does this happen? Have you ever thought about it?
2276 
2277Let's figure it out.
2278 
2279Factors to consider:
2280 
2281▪️ the presence of baking powder in the recipe
2282▪️ the presence of water in the recipe (in the composition of eggs)
2283▪️ the presence of protein and starch (in the recipe or in the composition of the baking powder)
2284▪️ incorporated air
2285▪️ heating to high temperatures
2286 
2287Now let’s have a closer look at each of the factors, step by step. Follow me.
2288 
2289🌬 By whipping the egg whites with sugar, we incorporate air into the meringue (and subsequently, into our sponge batter).
2290 
2291💧 When the baking powder is added along with the rest of the dry ingredients to the meringue, it reacts with the water contained in the eggs, and a neutralization reaction occurs, during which the carbon dioxide is released.
2292 
2293🔥Then, when we bake the sponge, this is what happens in the oven:
2294 
2295🗿 Under the influence of high temperatures, the process of protein and starch denaturation occurs, and they change from a liquid into a solid state
2296 
2297🫧 air bubbles, or bubbles of carbon dioxide, like any other gas, expand when heated (you might remember this from Physics lessons at school) and remain in the form of cavities in the sponge, in other words, make it porous, and after cooling, carbon dioxide and steam come out from these cavities, and they are filled with air
2298 
2299❌ If you open the oven while baking the sponge, the temperature in it will drop sharply, the gas will shrink back, the bubbles will become smaller, and if the protein has not yet had time to denature, the sponge will deflate.
2300LUMPY CREAM CHEESE MIXTURE: WHY DOES IT HAPPEN AND HOW TO PREVENT IT?
2301 
2302#marusya_theory_tips
2303 
2304Once, at a masterclass, I approached a student and, seeing her cheesecake mixture, asked: “Have you used a sieve?”, she replied: “Yes, how do you know?”
2305 
2306Guys, it’s very simple — even if you push a lumpy cream cheese mixture through a sieve, you still will have a lumpy mixture. The only difference will be the size of the lumps which will be equal to the diameter of the sieve mesh.
2307 
2308Therefore, there are 2 answers to the question of why the cheesecake mixture is lumpy:
2309 
2310✖️ the mixture wasn’t properly mixed
2311✖️ the mixture wasn’t properly mixed + you tried to fix it in the wrong way
2312 
2313To prevent lumps in the cheesecake mixture, one should adhere to the following rules ⬇️
2314 
2315☑️ Use a flex edge flat beater
2316 
2317Don’t mix the cheesecake mixture with a whisk attachment — firstly, the cream cheese will get caught up in the whisk’s wires and lumps will form; secondly, the whisk will incorporate lots of air into the mixture, and as a result, you won’t be able to fit all the mixture into the crust, so after baking the cheesecake will sink in the center.
2318 
2319📎 You can also use a paddle attachment, however, you’ll have to periodically stop the mixer and scrape the cream cheese from the sides of the bowl and the attachment itself, while the flex edge flat beater removes the mixture from the sides of the bowl during mixing
2320 
2321☑️ Stick to the sequence of adding the ingredients:
2322 
2323🔽 cream cheese
2324🔽 sugar
2325🔽 eggs
2326🔽 flavoring additives (purees, ganaches, pastes)
2327🔽 cream, milk, alcohol
2328 
2329First, mix the cream cheese and sugar (if listed in the recipe) into a pasty consistency. Next, pour in the egg melange in a thin stream, or add the egg yolks one at a time, mixing thoroughly after each addition.
2330 
2331🖊 Egg melange is a homogeneous mass of eggs combined with a whisk or blender
2332 
2333Then add the flavorings, and lastly — the liquid ingredients.
2334 
2335📎 if the flavorings have a very thick consistency, e.g. dulce de leche, thick nut paste, it’s better to add them before you start introducing the eggs
2336HONEY DOUGH: 3 CONSISTENCIES YOU SHOULD KNOW ABOUT
2337 
2338If you still think that a honey cake is an uninteresting dessert that can only amaze you with the number of cake layers, just scroll through my feed and the myth will be dispelled instantly.
2339 
2340How to get such appetizing cross sections?
2341 
2342One of the keys is the optimal consistency of honey dough for each particular honey cake. Let me cover the crucial questions.
2343 
2344What determines the consistency of honey dough?
2345✔️ the amount of flour in its composition — the less flour you use, the more liquid and / or sticky the dough will be
2346 
2347❓What are the types of honey dough consistencies?
2348✔️ 1. Stiff
2349✔️ 2. Soft
2350✔️ 3. Liquid
2351 
2352STIFF HONEY DOUGH:
2353 
2354▪️obtained by adding a moderate amount of flour
2355▪️should be slightly sticky when mixed
2356▪️ready to work with (!!!) only after sitting for 15–20 minutes at room temperature — the fats contained in butter stabilize and the dough becomes non-sticky
2357▪️rolled out with a rolling pin
2358▪️easy, almost effortless to roll out
2359 
2360SOFT HONEY DOUGH:
2361 
2362▪️used for cake layers with a reduced flour content
2363▪️turns out very sticky when mixed
2364▪️ready to work with after 4–12 hours in the fridge (depending on the recipe) — during this time the fats become solid and stabilize completely –> the dough becomes non-sticky
2365▪️easy to roll out with a rolling pin
2366 
2367LIQUID HONEY DOUGH (BATTER):
2368 
2369▪️contains the least amount of flour
2370▪️ready to work with immediately after mixing
2371▪️cannot be rolled out — for this batter you’ll need an offset spatula — evenly spread the batter over the parchment paper, giving it the desired shape
2372 
2373❓What does the consistency of honey dough affect?
2374✔️ the texture of finished cake layers
2375 
2376▪️ crispy and crumbly cake layers are obtained from stiff dough
2377▪️ soft dough ensures tender, soft and slightly airy cake layers
2378▪️ honey batter helps to get elastic and at the same time very pliable cake layers
2379TEMPERATURE FOR ADDING BUTTER TO CARAMEL
2380 
2381#marusya_theory_tips
2382 
2383Creamy caramel — a well-known to all confectioners and, seemingly, an easy-to-prepare French dessert, consisting of only 4 ingredients, in fact, is full of many secrets.
2384 
2385One of them is the temperature at which the butter is added to the caramel.
2386 
2387Let's have a look at 3 options, which I’ve tried and tested myself👌
2388 
2389🟠1. Adding the butter to caramel at 50 °C ensures
2390⬇️⬇️⬇️
2391🔸 the lightest shade
2392🔸 the thickest texture
2393🔸 rich creamy taste
2394🔸 pronounced buttery taste
2395🔸 mild caramel flavor
2396 
2397🟠 2. Adding the butter to caramel at 60 °C ensures
2398⬇️⬇️⬇️
2399🔸 caramel shade
2400🔸 nice soft texture
2401🔸 mild taste of butter or burnt sugar
2402🔸 perfectly balanced creamy caramel flavor
2403 
2404ℹ️ I normally add the butter at this temperature
2405 
2406🟠 3. Adding the butter to caramel at 170 °C (right into the dry caramel, before adding the cream) ensures
2407⬇️⬇️⬇️
2408🔸 the darkest shade ⬅️ the temperature doesn’t drop as sharply as with the addition of cream
2409🔸 gooey texture, somewhat reminiscent of toffee
2410🔸 intense caramel flavor
2411🔸 nutty flavor ⬅️ the butter melts fast and boils, and as a result it acquires nutty notes, as in the preparation of beurre noisette
2412 
2413ℹ️ you can read more about the beurre noisette by following the hashtag #marusya_beurre_noisette
2414WHAT YOU NEED TO KNOW ABOUT MOUSSES
2415part 1
2416 
2417#marusya_about_mousse
2418 
2419Guys, today I’m presenting to you a new series of posts. Let's learn the science of making mousses from A to Z so you can feel confident when making them.
2420 
2421Let's start, as always, with the basics. What is a mousse?
2422 
2423Mousse (from French 'mousse' – “foam”) is a dish or a filling for cakes and other desserts with the consistency of an airy, light foam, composed of:
2424 
24251️⃣ flavoring component (base)
24262️⃣ whipped component (aerator)
24273️⃣ thickener
2428 
2429Now let's take a closer look at each component.
2430 
2431✅ MOUSSE BASE is the main flavoring component which can be:
2432 
2433🔸 custard
2434🔸 ganache
2435🔸 fruit puree
2436🔸 creamy caramel
2437🔸 nut or flower paste
2438🔸 yogurt or fermented baked milk (ryazhenka)
2439🔸 coffee/tea/alcohol
2440 
2441There are mousses with a “pure” base, i.e. only one ingredient is used, for example, caramel. But in my opinion, the most interesting are mousses with complex bases – you can combine coffee with fruit puree, caramel with nut paste or even crème anglaise with alcohol!
2442 
2443The mousse base takes up ~35-55% of the total mass, depending on the thickness of the base and the desired consistency of the finished mousse.
2444 
2445✅ WHIPPED COMPONENT OR AERATOR is responsible for airiness and lightness of the mousse. These aerators can be:
2446 
2447🔸 whipping cream
2448🔸 meringue
2449🔸 pâte à bombe
2450 
2451🖊 Pâte à bombe – a cream made of whipped egg yolks and boiled sugar syrup.
2452 
2453Before being added to the mousse, any of these ingredients is whipped to incorporate air bubbles, which helps to make the product airy and fluffy.
2454 
2455Meringue-based mousses
2456 
2457🔺most often, the Italian meringue is used, as it’s the most stable of all types of meringue
2458🔺contain the greatest number of air bubbles, and therefore are the most unstable, soft and delicate
2459🔺 have the shortest shelf life
2460🔺 keep maximum taste of the flavoring base
2461part 2
2462 
2463#marusya_about_mousse
2464 
2465Thanks to a large number of pluses under yesterday's post, I decided to immediately write a continuation – it's always nice to see mutual interest❤️
2466 
2467Cream-based mousses
2468 
2469🔺 make the taste of the mousse base less prominent, unlike meringue-based mousses
2470🔺 more stable and thicker than those made with meringue
2471🔺 use cold 30–33% cream
2472🔺 whip it into soft peaks at medium speed – fast whipping leads to a less stable foam
2473🔺 stick to the whipping temperature – 0–5 °C – if the temperature is at least 3–4 °C higher, the cream will whip much worse or may even curdle
2474🔺 it’s important not to over-whip the cream, otherwise it’ll be difficult to combine it with the base, and the texture of the mousse won’t be perfect
2475🔺 the temperature of the base to which we add the whipped cream shouldn’t exceed 35 °C, otherwise the mousse will turn out to be too liquid
2476 
2477It’s not common to use pure pâte à bombe to make mousses – most often it’s combined with cream or meringue.
2478 
2479In general, in one mousse you can use:
2480 
2481▪️whipped cream and meringue
2482▪️meringue and pâte à bombe
2483▪️whipped cream and pâte à bombe
2484▪️whipped cream, meringue and pâte à bombe
2485 
2486The whipped component can take up around 35–60% of the total mass of the mousse, depending on the thickness of the base, the type of aeration and the desired consistency of the finished mousse.
2487 
2488✅ THICKENER: traditionally, mousses are made with gelatin. However, some of the recipes call for agar-agar or cocoa butter, or gelatin + cocoa butter. The more gelatin you add, the thicker the mousse will be, and vice versa.
2489 
2490Gelatin can take up 0.5–2% of the total mass of the mousse:
2491 
2492▪️ if the mousse contains alcohol, tart fruits or lots of air (meringue-based mousses), the percentage of gelatin will be around 2%
2493▪️ mousses made from milk or white couvertures (chocolate with a high content of cocoa butter), contain around 0.5% gelatin
2494▪️if it’s a chocolate-based mousse which contains enough cocoa butter (couverture/dark/bitter chocolate), you can skip adding the gelatin
2495PISTACHIO PASTE: 2 TIPS YOU DIDN'T KNOW ABOUT
2496 
2497Pistachio paste has been added to all the desserts you see in the carousel, whether it be crémeux, cream for coating, cream cheese layer or frosting used between layers.
2498 
2499For me, it was extremely important to get a beautiful pistachio color and, at the same time, a pronounced yet natural pistachio taste.
2500 
2501How have I achieved this?
2502 
2503Let me share my 2 little tips
2504 
2505🌿 TIP #1
2506 
2507✅ Combination of natural pistachio paste with pistachio delipaste
2508 
2509A natural nutty taste is obtained using natural pistachio paste that has 100% pistachio in the composition. But most often, it has an unappetizing greenish brown color, so it cannot give the product the very beautiful pistachio color that instantly makes us want to try the dessert.
2510 
2511On the contrary, pistachio delipaste (with only ~ 7% pistachio in the composition) contains a natural dye chlorophyll, which gives a beautiful pistachio color to products. What is more, the addition of delipaste balances out the slight bitterness of natural pistachio paste, and you get a pleasant taste reminiscent of pistachio ice cream.
2512 
2513🖊delipaste is a concentrated flavoring paste that gives a pronounced taste, aroma and color of a certain product to a dessert.
2514 
2515🌿 TIP #2
2516 
2517✅Natural paste and delipaste ratio is 1:1 or 2:1
2518 
2519You can adjust the amount of paste added depending on what you want to emphasize more — taste or color.
2520 
2521🌿 BRANDS I USE (*BONUS)
2522 
2523✅ Pastes — Joygelato, ТМ Irca (Italy) or NutFarine (Ukraine)
2524✅ Delipaste — Fabbri (Italy)
2525HOW TO TEMPER EGGS AND WHY YOU NEED TO🥚🌡
2526part 1
2527 
2528#marusya_theory_tips
2529 
2530Every year I become more convinced that eggs are one of the most interesting products in the confectionery world!
2531 
2532This post is about egg tempering. I'll tell you how to temper the eggs and, more importantly, why we need to.
2533 
2534I think you immediately started to wonder:
2535 
2536❓ Tempering eggs? How come? It's all about chocolate!
2537✔️ In fact, in the confectionery world, the word "tempering" has 2 meanings:
2538 
2539🌡 1st — it’s the process of heating and cooling chocolate to certain temperatures in order to obtain stable cocoa butter crystals responsible for giving the chocolate a glossy look and a nice crunch;
2540 
2541🌡 2d — it’s the process of slowly heating the eggs.
2542 
2543Scientifically, this method can be described as follows:
2544 
2545TEMPERING EGGS means slowly combining beaten cold or room-temperature eggs with a hot liquid without scrambling or curdling the eggs in the process.
2546 
2547HOW TO TEMPER EGGS (IN BRIEF)
2548 
25491. Slowly stream the hot/boiling liquid into the cold/room-temperature eggs while whisking.
25502. Pour the warm eggs back into the saucepan.
2551part 2
2552 
2553#marusya_theory_tips
2554 
2555In a previous post, we discussed with you what tempering eggs is. So where does this process take place?
2556 
2557In fact, it’s quite simple and, most likely, you have already tempered eggs without knowing it. We temper eggs when we prepare the base for custards, pudding, ice cream, flan, crème brûlée, etc.
2558 
2559In the preparation of any custard, for example, there’s a step of trickling hot milk or cream into the egg mixture — when gradually adding a hot liquid to the eggs, we thereby temper them.
2560 
2561🔴 WHY WE NEED TO TEMPER EGGS
2562 
2563Tempering eggs prevents the proteins from coagulating. Eggs are very sensitive to temperature. If you combine the eggs with hot liquid in one go, they will curdle. Therefore, when tempering, 2 rules must be observed:
2564 
2565✔️ add the hot liquid gradually, in a thin stream
2566✔️ constantly stir the mixture with a whisk
2567 
2568Lastly, let’s analyze one more interesting nuance.
2569 
2570🔴 HOW MUCH HOT LIQUID SHOULD BE ADDED TO EGGS
2571 
2572Tempering is a means of getting something (eggs) from point A to point B (from cold to hot) slowly and steadily. And is a technique, not a scientific formula, which is why there’s no definitive answer for this question.
2573 
2574But there is no need to add ✖️all the hot liquid✖️ to the eggs — you should add just enough liquid to make the eggs hot (not warm, but hot!). You can then safely pour the tempered eggs into the saucepan with the remaining hot liquid. Just do not forget to stir with a whisk until the mixture becomes homogeneous.
2575HYDROCOLLOIDS IN CONFECTIONERY ART
2576part 1
2577 
2578#marusya_hydrocolloids
2579 
2580The other day, while searching for useful information about the functions of sugar in shortcrust pastry, I came across an interesting block "Modern techniques in confectionery art", devoted to hydrocolloids.
2581 
2582Let's figure out what they are.
2583 
2584Basically, hydrocolloids are a heterogeneous group of long chain polymers (polysaccharides and proteins) characterized by their property of forming viscous dispersions and/or gels when dispersed in water.
2585 
2586It may sound confusing, but most of them are familiar to you. In simple terms, hydrocolloids are stabilizers and thickeners, derived from animal and plant sources.
2587 
2588Hydrocolloids of animal origin include the well-known gelatin. But I suggest having a closer look at hydrocolloids of vegetable origin.
2589 
2590Hydrocolloids derived from plant sources include:
2591🌱agar-agar
2592🌱pectin
2593🌱carrageenans
2594🌱gellan gum
2595🌱metilgel/methylcellulose
2596🌱sodium alginate
2597🌱tapioca maltodextrin
2598🌱xanthan gum
2599🌱guar gum
2600 
2601I think most of you only know the agar-agar and pectin from this list.
2602 
2603Pastry enthusiasts obsessed with confectionery could have seen the rest on the labels of bright, colorful and alluring SOSA bottles.
2604 
2605Or on white, plain and strict but no less attractive large containers with a red lid from LOUIS FRANCOIS, arranged in beautiful rows in the DGF boutique.
2606 
2607Those who are not into the confectionery business and are not familiar with the brands mentioned above, may remember some of the stabilizers and thickeners from the compositions of dairy and meat products.
2608 
2609So why, where and how are hydrocolloids used in modern confectionery production?
2610 
2611First of all, they are used as thickeners, of course, — to obtain gels, jellies, sauces, jams and other stable fillings. This is well known to everyone. But few people know that with the help of vegetable hydrocolloids, you can make ...
2612 
2613Can you guess the answer? Type it in the comments section.
2614 
2615P.S. Let me give you a little hint — hydrocolloids are often used in modern molecular cuisine 🤫
2616part 2
2617 
2618#marusya_hydrocolloids
2619 
2620Judging by the answers in the comments section under the previous part of this post, you don’t know much about the application of hydrocolloids in modern cuisine. Let's try to change this.
2621 
2622So, here is a list of the most interesting and unusual, in my opinion, applications of plant-derived hydrocolloids:
2623 
2624🌱foams – vary from thick and rich (like shaving foam) to light "soapy" foams with fruit, milk or coffee taste
2625🌱 crispy dry foams made from milk and juices
2626🌱 "hot ice cream" that melts when it starts cooling
2627🌱 "caviar" – semi-solid beads with thin membranes out of liquids
2628🌱 "raw egg yolk" made from mango or apricot puree – a large sphere with thin gel-like membrane out of liquid
2629🌱 dry caramel, nut paste
2630🌱 gluten-free flour.
2631 
2632I’m curious to know which of the above applications of hydrocolloids interested you the most? Type in the comments. If there are many comments, I will share a recipe 😉
2633 
2634In the meantime, let's get acquainted with the most mysterious representative of hydrocolloids of vegetable origin.
2635 
2636🔴 Carrageenan
2637✔️extracted from some red seaweeds
2638✔️thermally reversible
2639✔️dissolves in cold water when mixed with sugar
2640✔️melting point 80ºC
2641✔️setting point 70ºC
2642✔️catalyst – calcium / potassium (usually in dairy products)
2643 
2644Functions:
2645🌱 turns sauces into gels (rigid or elastic, tough or tender)
2646 
2647🌱 increases the viscosity of liquids – is used, for example, in soy milk to give it a milk-like texture (without carrageenan it would have the consistency of water)
2648 
2649🌱 suspends particles in liquid – is used, for example, in chocolate milk, so that solid cocoa particles do not settle at the bottom
2650 
2651🌱 is used as a vegetable substitute for gelatin to stabilize mousses and frostings (mix the carrageenan powder with sugar and add to a cold liquid, then bring it to a boil, pour into molds and allow to stabilize)
2652EVERYTHING YOU NEED TO KNOW ABOUT GANASHES
2653part 1
2654 
2655#marusya_about_ganache
2656 
2657I wanted to post a brief instruction on how to make chocolate ganache, but when I started writing, I realized that this topic requires more than one post. So welcome the new series of posts😉
2658 
2659Let's start, as always, with the basics.
2660 
2661⚫️What is ganache?
2662 
2663Ganache is a mixture (emulsion) of chocolate (white, milk or dark) and liquid.
2664 
2665Ganache is made using a hot method — you heat the liquid and pour it over chocolate, after which you allow the ganache to cool.
2666 
2667What exactly is meant by liquid?
2668 
2669If on the Internet you come across an article that says that there are three types of ganache — made with heavy cream, butter and heavy cream with the addition of butter — you can immediately close the tab, since the author has only superficial knowledge about such an interesting product as ganache.
2670 
2671Firstly, the liquid in ganache can be represented by:
2672 
2673▪️milk
2674▪️heavy cream
2675▪️fruit or berry juice
2676▪️fruit or berry puree
2677▪️and even crème anglaise or other custard
2678 
2679Secondly, in some cases, you can add to your ganache the following ingredients:
2680 
2681▪️butter
2682▪️sugar
2683▪️all kinds of flavoring additives
2684 
2685Let’s continue.
2686 
2687Depending on the texture and purpose, the ganache can be thick or more runny:
2688 
2689▪️ runny — is used for tarts and chocolates
2690▪️ soft — can be used as an interlayer or filling for cakes, individual cakes or desserts
2691▪️ thick — is used for bonbons and cake coating
2692 
2693The texture of the ganache can also be changed — it depends on the way you stir it — e.g, ganache can be whipped or made using the Chantilly cream method.
2694 
2695Ganache is a stable and balanced emulsion type chain in which fat molecules (in particular cocoa butter, milk fat, milk and/or heavy cream) are combined into a homogeneous substance together with water molecules thanks to emulsifiers present in chocolate (lecithin) and in heavy cream (proteins).
2696A CRASH COURSE IN CHOCOLATE
2697part 1
2698 
2699Hello everyone! I have great news for you - today we are launching a series of articles about chocolate!
2700 
2701Even not being a chocolatier, almost every confectioner encounters chocolate when making cake decorating, ganaches, frostings, mousses, cremeux and other fillings. Therefore, acquaintance and building friendly relationships with a product such as chocolate will be relevant for many of you.
2702 
2703Let's start with the basics.
2704 
2705WHAT IS CHOCOLATE?
2706 
2707Chocolate is a confectionery product based on cocoa butter, which is a product of processing cocoa beans - the seeds of the chocolate tree, which are rich in theobromine and caffeine.
2708 
2709TYPES OF CHOCOLATE AND ITS COMPOSITION
2710 
27111. Black
27122. Milk
27133. White
2714 
2715The division is based on differences in composition, which determines the appearance of the product and its taste.
2716 
2717COMPOSITION OF BLACK CHOCOLATE
2718 
2719- cocoa mass
2720- cocoa butter
2721- sugar
2722 
2723COMPOSITION OF MILK CHOCOLATE
2724 
2725- cocoa mass
2726- cocoa butter
2727- sugar
2728- milk powder
2729 
2730COMPOSITION OF WHITE CHOCOLATE
2731 
2732- cocoa butter
2733- sugar
2734- milk powder
2735 
2736*In fact, white chocolate isn’t really chocolate, as it doesn’t contain cocoa mass (cocoa beans). On this basis, the debate about whether to consider it real chocolate is still ongoing. I work with it all the time and refer it to chocolate, as due to the presence of cocoa butter in its composition, it has all the properties of chocolate.
2737 
2738WHAT ELSE CAN BE IN THE COMPOSITION OF CHOCOLATE:
2739 
2740In order for all the components to combine into a smooth emulsion, a small percentage of an emulsifier, such as soy lecithin, is often added to chocolate.
2741 
2742WHAT YOU SHOULD NOT HAVE IN CHOCOLATE:
2743 
2744Good chocolate should be free of vegetable oils, vanillin and any other by-products.
2745 
2746And now the SECRET INFORMATION which is in open access 😁
2747 
2748🟤 The percentage indicated on the chocolate packaging (70%, 54.5%, 33%) is the percentage of cocoa mass.
2749 
2750❗️BUT❗️
2751 
2752⚪️ The percentage indicated on the WHITE chocolate packaging is the percentage of cocoa butter content.
2753part 2
2754 
2755Let's continue delving into the world of chocolate. In the previous post you’ve asked about the brands that I use, so I’ll tell you what I usually work with.
2756 
2757The main rule when choosing chocolate is to give preference to PROFESSIONAL CHOCOLATE to get a stable result. You can easily buy it at any confectionery store.
2758 
2759If any of you still think that professional chocolate is a hard-to-find and expensive product, I have good news for you. Currently, the professional chocolate market is represented by a huge number of brands, among which everyone can find an attractive price option.
2760 
2761It can be:
2762 
2763🔸 IRCA (Italy)
2764🔸 DGF (France)
2765🔸 Natra Cacao (Spain)
2766🔸 Cacao Barry (France)
2767🔸 Lubeca (Germany)
2768🔸 Callebaut (Belgium)
2769 
2770Many chefs prefer a French premium chocolate Valrhona, known for its line of chocolate with unique flavors. Desserts and even whole lines of desserts based on Valrhona chocolate are being developed, as they already contain certain flavors and don’t need to add anything extra - neither food dyes, nor puree, nor any other supplements. Of course, it optimizes many processes.
2771 
2772Since the fact that not everyone has the opportunity to use such expensive chocolate, I prefer DGF or Callebaut chocolate. Though I also enjoy working with brands such as IRCA and Lubeca.
2773 
2774I'll tell you more about Callebaut.
2775 
2776It is Belgian chocolate with a well-adjusted balanced flavor and texture made exclusively using natural ingredients. It is pleasant and easy to work with it, due to its high quality, as well as the convenient form of callets (drops), which is universal for almost any confectionery purpose.
2777 
2778🌿 The line of «Finest Belgian Chocolate» is fully handmade, from cacao beans roasting to chocolate
2779part 3
2780 
2781In the previous posts — #marusya_about_chocolate — we have covered the basics: definition of chocolate, its types, composition and brands.
2782 
2783With this knowledge, we can proceed to the next step — tempering.
2784 
2785WHAT IS TEMPERING?
2786 
2787Tempering, or crystallization, is the process of heating and cooling chocolate to certain temperatures in order to obtain stable cocoa butter crystals, which gives the chocolate a number of important properties:
2788 
2789✔️gives chocolate a glossy finish and make it snap cleanly
2790✔️allows you to avoid undesirable imperfections on the surface of chocolate:
2791▪️ sugar bloom — a whitish film what affects the tasting
2792▪️ fat bloom — greyish or whitish streaks or spots of cocoa butter
2793✔️chocolate hardens much faster
2794✔️ stays solid in warmer temperatures, as it has a higher melting point.
2795 
2796METHODS FOR TEMPERING CHOCOLATE:
2797 
2798✔️classic (tabling)
2799✔️seeding (callets)
2800✔️Mycryo — powdered cocoa butter
2801 
2802CLASSIC TEMPERING METHOD
2803 
2804The method requires you to use a marble or granite slab/countertop. Since not everyone has an access to it, I won’t dwell on it in detail. But the technique itself is important for understanding the processes:
2805 
2806Destroy the solid form of chocolate by bringing it to the melting point:
2807▪️dark — 50-55°C
2808▪️milk — 45-50°C
2809▪️white — 40-45°C
2810 
2811✔️Bring it down to the crystallization temperature of cocoa butter — 27°C
2812 
2813✔️Bring the chocolate to its working temperature:
2814▪️dark — 31-32°C
2815▪️milk — 30-31°C
2816▪️white — 29-30°C
2817 
2818✔️Give liquid chocolate the desired shape.
2819 
2820✔️Let the chocolate stabilize and become solid again.
2821 
2822SEEDING METHOD OR CALLET TEMPERING
2823 
2824The trick is that the callets themselves are already tempered. Cocoa butter crystals are already crystallized, that is, step 2 from the classic method is omitted. All you need to do is melt the chocolate and bring it to working temperature.
2825part 4
2826 
2827We continue a detailed analysis of the methods for tempering chocolate.
2828 
2829As you remember from the previous post — #marusya_about_chocolate — the classic method is not the easiest one, since it requires lots of effort as well as marble or granite slab/countertop. But at the same time, this is the only way to temper chocolate in large quantities (over 1 kg).
2830 
2831In large scale production where it’s often necessary to temper 1-2 kg of chocolate or even more, they use special tempering machines that maintain the required temperature of chocolate.
2832 
2833For home bakers, one of the best methods is the seeding method or callet tempering. Let’s learn how it works.
2834 
2835SEEDING METHOD OR CALLET TEMPERING
2836 
2837Destroy the solid form of chocolate by bringing it to the melting point:
2838▪️dark — 50–55 °C
2839▪️milk — 45–50 °C
2840▪️white — 40–45 °C
2841 
2842✔️Add chocolate callets and stir to bring the chocolate to its working temperature:
2843▪️dark — 31–32 °C
2844▪️milk — 30–31 °C
2845▪️white — 29–30 °C
2846 
2847And voilà — you can give liquid chocolate the desired shape👌
2848 
2849With this method there is no need to first bring chocolate down to the crystallization temperature of cocoa butter (27 °C), and then bring it up to the working temperature, as with the classic method, since the callets themselves are already tempered and have a stable crystalline structure.
2850 
2851❓If I use chocolate callets only, then I just melt them and that's it?
2852 
2853❌ No!
2854 
2855Even if you use only chocolate in callets, bringing it to working temperature is not enough — heat treatment destroys the bonds between cocoa butter molecules, the crystalline structure breaks up and collapses.
2856 
2857That’s why it is necessary to add cold callets with a stable crystalline structure to melted chocolate, as they prompt the chain reaction resulting in properly crystallized chocolate.
2858part 5
2859 
2860Secrets of tempering chocolate using seeding method (callets)
2861 
28621️⃣ It is best to melt chocolate in the microwave. Chocolate is very hygroscopic, meaning it absorbs moisture from the air. Therefore, a water bath will not be the best option for melting chocolate.
2863 
28642️⃣ To avoid overheating, melt the chocolate in the microwave in 20 to 30 second intervals, thoroughly stirring it with a spatula between each interval.
2865 
2866📍 melt the white chocolate on the lowest power level and in very short, 10-20 second intervals as it can be easily overheated
2867 
28683️⃣ I do not recommend melting chocolate in glassware — glass retains temperature for a long time and gives it to chocolate even after we’ve already brought it to 50-55 °C (45-50 °C milk and 40-45 °C white chocolate). Opt for plastic vessels instead.
2869 
28704️⃣ Chocolate must be constantly stirred! This is necessary in order to ensure the same temperature of chocolate at the bottom, sides and in the middle of the bowl, as well as to correctly measure the temperature. It is most convenient to measure the temperature of chocolate with a pyrometer.
2871 
28725️⃣ In order to bring the chocolate to its working temperature (dark — 31-32 °C, milk — 30-31 °C, white — 29-30 °C), add callets to the melted chocolate (20-25% of the total volume). With this amount, stable crystals will prompt the chain reaction resulting in properly crystallized chocolate.
2873 
2874This ratio is considered optimal, but since there are many variables in this equation, I would call it only approximate. You can be guided by it, but make sure to always keep an eye on the temperature of the chocolate.
2875 
28766️⃣ When you get down to work, please remember that:
2877 
2878🔺 You need to work with chocolate quickly — if the chocolate is properly tempered, it will set in 3 minutes at a temperature of about 20 °C
2879🔺 white chocolate is much thicker than dark or milk chocolate — to make it easier to work with, add a little cocoa butter along with the callets to it
2880part 6
2881 
2882#marusya_about_chocolate
2883 
2884Today I suggest talking about a very interesting product, which has no analogues on the market at the moment — Mycryo.
2885 
2886🟤 WHAT IS MYCRYO?
2887 
2888Mycryo® is the brand name of the Barry Callebaut group of companies, which specializes in the production of a unique product — pure cocoa butter in powder form.
2889 
2890This 100% vegetable fat is an absolutely healthy product, mostly thanks to cocoa beans: it contains no cholesterol, has low calorie content, and you need less fat for frying.
2891 
2892If you look at the original packaging of Mycryo, you will see a photo of a delicious steak. And there’s a reason for that. Mycryo has a number of unique properties that make the range of its application very diverse:
2893 
2894🔺 it resists frying temperatures better than butter or oils, and doesn't burn
2895🔺brings out the natural flavors of even the most delicate ingredients, and unlike other fats, it does not dominate over them
2896🔺 and, of course, facilitates the process of chocolate tempering
2897 
2898🟤 HOW IS MYCRYO OBTAINED?
2899 
2900Cocoa butter, obtained through roasting and grinding cocoa beans, is then frozen at very low temperatures to produce a dry powder. In other words, atomization occurs — dry powder is cooled to approximately -250 °C.
2901 
2902As a result, cocoa butter takes the form of granules, which are pure beta crystals, which are essential for proper chocolate tempering.
2903 
2904Let’s try to figure it out.
2905 
2906🟤 TYPES OF COCOA BUTTER CRYSTALS
2907 
2908So, there are six types of cocoa butter crystals, each with a name: γ‎ (gamma), α (alpha), β'' (beta 2), β' (beta prime), β (beta) and super-β (super-beta).
2909 
2910They differ from each other by the strength of the bonds between the molecules. The stronger the bonds are, the more energy (higher temperature) is needed to break these bonds.
2911 
2912Of all six crystals, only one fits for tempering — beta (β) — they are the most stable and form the correct crystal lattice.
2913part 7
2914 
2915#marusya_about_chocolate
2916 
2917Chocolate tempering with Mycryo — the conventional technique VS method I use.
2918 
2919You asked me for the details, so there you are.
2920 
2921🟤 CONVENTIONAL TECHNIQUE FOR CHOCOLATE TEMPERING WITH MYCRYO
2922 
29231️⃣ Melt the chocolate to 40-45 °C in a microwave oven or over a water bath.
29242️⃣ Leave the chocolate to cool (to 34-35 °C for dark chocolate or 33-34 °C for milk, white or colored chocolate) at room temperature.
29253️⃣ Add Mycryo (1% of the chocolate mass), mix thoroughly.
29264️⃣ Once the chocolate has reached an ideal temperature (31-32 °C for dark chocolate or 29-30 °C for milk, white or colored chocolate), use the product.
29275️⃣ To use the chocolate for a longer time, keep it at 31-32 °C in the case of dark chocolate or 29-30 °C in the case of milk, white or colored chocolate.
2928 
2929🟤 MY CHOCOLATE TEMPERING METHOD USING MYCRYO
2930 
29311️⃣ Melt the chocolate to 40-45 °C in the microwave
2932❌ It is better not to use a water bath — chocolate is very hygroscopic
2933 
29342️⃣ Add ~15-20% callets of the total chocolate mass and mix thoroughly until they have completely dissolved, lowering the temperature to 35 °C.
2935❌Cooling chocolate at room temperature takes a long time and it must be constantly stirred, otherwise the crystallization will be uneven — chocolate cools much faster on the walls of the bowl.
2936 
2937❗️3️⃣ Sift Mycryo through a fine sieve to get rid of large pieces — when adding Mycryo, the temperature of the chocolate drops rapidly, so you need to combine the chocolate with cocoa butter into a homogeneous mass very quickly, which makes it difficult for large pieces of Mycryo to get in.
2938 
29394️⃣ Add Mycryo (2% of the chocolate mass), stir thoroughly.
2940❌ Earlier on the official website it was indicated that 1% Mycryo is enough, but recently the manufacturer has changed the proportion to 2% — that is, 2 g of Mycryo per 100 g of chocolate.
2941 
2942✅5️⃣ see step 4 of the conventional technique
2943✅6️⃣ see step 5 of the conventional technique
2944ALL YOU NEED TO KNOW ABOUT STARCH
2945 
2946#marusya_about _starch
2947 
2948We’ve already talked about pectin, agar-agar and gelatin. So today I suggest talking about the most popular thickening agent — starch.
2949 
2950In fact, there are lots of types of starch: potato, rice, wheat, corn, tapioca, sorghum, etc. However, the most common types are potato and cornstarch.
2951 
2952It is preferable to use cornstarch for making desserts, and it’s not recommended to replace it with potato starch. Cornstarch ensures a more delicate texture, while adding potato starch results in a much thicker texture.
2953 
2954Potato starch
2955🔸 ensures a more fluid texture
2956🔸 has a characteristic flavor
2957🔸 the starch mixture is transparent
2958🔸 may impart a bluish color to some dishes
2959 
2960Cornstarch
2961🔸 ensures a more viscous texture
2962🔸 has no aftertaste
2963🔸 the starch mixture is turbid
2964🔸 does not change the color of the dish
2965 
2966Types of cornstarch
2967✔️regular cornstarch — does not contain impurities and has a white color
2968✔️modified starch — a safe product, additionally processed for better absorption
2969 
2970Take into account that❗️starch as a thickener activates properly only after bringing the mixture to a boil❗️
2971 
2972The boiling point of mixtures with starch added is 82 °C.
2973 
2974Bring the filling or custard with cornstarch to a boil, then, stirring vigorously with a spatula, allow it to cook over low heat for several minutes so that the starch has completely denatured — subjected to a heat treatment, the starch granules should swell and change their structure.
2975 
2976If the starch has not completely swollen, after cooling, the filling won’t be stable and may leak water, acquiring a liquid, runny structure.
2977 
2978The dosage of starch may be different depending on:
2979 
2980✔️ density of the product to be thickened: liquid with a high percentage of water or a thicker puree with a high content of natural pectin
2981✔️ consistency of the filling you want to get
2982HOW TO CORRECTLY MAKE AND SERVE A CAKE WITH BUTTERCREAM
2983 
2984#marusya_theory_tips
2985 
2986It hasn’t been long since a new Napoleon cake recipe set was released, and I’ve already received lots of similar feedback: “The cake is delicious, but I didn’t like the buttercream — it’s heavy and too greasy.”
2987 
2988❓Can Napoleons and other cakes with buttercream taste like this?
2989✅ Yes, they can
2990 
2991❓Is there some mistake in the recipe?
2992❌ No, there isn’t
2993 
2994There are 2 reasons why your buttercream can turn out to be heavy and greasy:
2995 
29961️⃣ You didn’t follow the buttercream preparation instructions properly
29972️⃣ You served the cake with buttercream inappropriately
2998 
2999So, let's learn how to get the best results.
3000 
3001HOW TO MAKE THE PERFECT BUTTERCREAM:
3002 
3003🔸 make sure your butter is soft
3004🔸 whip the butter on maximum speed of your mixer
3005🔸 the whipping time should be from 3–5 to 10–15 minutes, depending on the power of your mixer and the amount of butter (it’s impossible to over whip the buttercream)
3006🔸 if you’re making a cake for yourself, I advise you to try using 72% butter instead of 82% — this way you will get a lighter version of the buttercream
3007 
3008➡️At a result, properly whipped butter should increase in volume by 2 times and become lighter. Well whipped butter will allow you to get a light, airy and not greasy buttercream.
3009 
3010HOW SHOULD THE CAKE WITH BUTTERCREAM BE SERVED:
3011 
3012🔸 in no case do we serve the cake immediately from the fridge, as the fat molecules harden at this temperature
3013🔸 you should take a well stabilized cake out of the fridge and slice it into portions
3014🔸 keep the slices at room temperature for 10 to 30 minutes, depending on the temperature in the room, so that the buttercream softens
3015🔸 and only then serve the cake to the client, guest or enjoy the cake yourself
3016 
3017➡️ A properly served cake keeps its shape well, and has a light, airy buttercream
3018FLAVOR PAIRING. SOUR CHERRY 🍒
3019 
3020#marusya_flavor_pairing
3021 
3022The last time we analyzed the flavor of bilberries was more than a month ago. I think it's time to reveal all the flavor pairing secrets of sour cherries, which are relevant in the cold season.
3023 
3024🌿 Flavor description
3025🔸 tart / sweet and sour
3026🔸 refreshing
3027🔸 herbaceous and astringent
3028🔸 top fruity notes
3029🔸 almond notes
3030🔸 slightly astringent aftertaste
3031 
3032Sour cherries have a fresh, green, fruity flavor character. Their peculiarity is that they taste a little like almonds because of the pits. This taste is most easily revealed when you boil the sour cherries along with the pits.
3033 
3034📍 after such an experiment, it will no longer seem strange to you that a compound called benzaldehyde is used as an ingredient in cherry and other natural fruit flavors due to its bitter almond odor
3035 
3036🌿 Aroma Profile
3037🔸 floral fragrance
3038🔸 spicy vanilla notes
3039🔸 noble almond notes
3040🔸 light herbaceousness of the peel
3041🔸 moderate tannic qualities
3042 
3043Pairs well with:
3044 
3045🔻 spices and herbs — cloves, coriander, sage and thyme. Sour cherry perfectly goes with spices such as cinnamon and vanilla, and it is no coincidence that these combinations are included in cherry liqueurs.
3046 
3047🔻 other fruits and berries such as apricot, melon, yuzu, orange, peach, quince and plum
3048 
3049The 🍒 cherry-coconut pair deserves special attention — both of the fruits are representatives of stone fruits, in the dominant creamy and nutty flavors of which you can find some fruity notes, which makes their combination especially successful
3050 
3051🔻 Chocolate-sour cherry is always a winning combination! Sour cherries go well with both milk and dark chocolate. But bitter chocolate adds special elegance and nobility.
3052 
3053🔻 nuts — pecans, hazelnuts, walnuts and, of course, almonds.
3054 
3055The flavor combination 🍒 sour cherry-pistachio is also particularly noteworthy — separately, these are two completely different products: they have nothing in common in shape, taste, color or texture, but despite everything, together they create a truly amazing combination.
3056HONEY. MYTHS VS REALITY
3057 
3058part 1
3059 
3060Since without honey a honey cake is no longer a honey cake, I’ve been working with this product a lot for at least several years.
3061⠀
3062Therefore, I’ve faced a wide range of questions regarding this ingredient. The most popular of them are the following:
3063⠀
3064🌿 Can honey be heated? Doesn’t it become toxic after this?
3065⠀
3066🌿 What are the substitutes for honey in recipes and how to use them correctly?
3067⠀
3068⠀
3069Honey is a healthy and natural product. It contains glucose, fructose, a small amount of sucrose, flavonoids, microelements and enzymes. It has bactericidal and medicinal properties.
3070⠀
3071⠀
3072📌 So, the first question and the most common myth at the same time.
3073⠀
3074❗️ When heated, honey becomes toxic.
3075⠀
3076Honey won’t become toxic, no matter how long you heat it: it does not initially contain any toxic components that are dangerous to humans.
3077⠀
3078In fact, an organic compound known as 5-hydroxymethylfurfural (HMF) is formed when honey is heated. There is an opinion that this compound is highly toxic, that's why it’s dangerous to heat honey.
3079⠀
3080Let's see how toxic this compound is, and whether we risk to get food poisoning from eating a piece of honey cake or adding honey to hot tea.
3081⠀
3082HMF is an intermediate product of the breakdown of sugars in an acidic medium, which increases with heating, that is, HMF appears in absolutely all products where sugars have undergone heat treatment in the presence of acid.
3083⠀
3084Therefore, HMF is found in any baked goods, jams, roasted apples and beets, roasted coffee, soda, and often in quantities ten times greater than in honey.
3085⠀
3086⠀
3087Why, then, is there GOST (State Standard) that regulates the norm of HMF in honey? - let’s figure it out in the continuation of the article 😉
3088⠀
3089⠀
3090P.S. The photos show a honey cheesecake "Baklava". And yes, there is a lot of honey in it and it was baked at 170-180 °С / 338-356 °F 😅
3091part 2
3092⠀
3093So, we already know that an organic compound 5-hydroxymethylfurfural (HMF) is formed when honey is heated and it’s neither toxic, nor carcinogenic.
3094⠀
3095So why are there GOST (State Standard) recommendations according to which the HMF concentration in honey should not exceed 25 mg/kg ?!
3096⠀
3097The limit for oxy methyl furfural in honey was set not to protect people from a "toxic" product, but to ensure that the product has not undergone overheating during processing. ⠀
3098 
3099This indicator is mainly used by honey processors when purchasing lots of honey and helps to determine its age, as well as whether this honey has been overheated during presale processing.
3100⠀
3101Deviation from the norm characterizes not the danger of heated honey, but rather its uselessness, since enzymes and proteins denaturation takes place much earlier than HMF appears. In this sense, those who like to put honey into hot tea have nothing to worry about, except that honey will lose its medicinal properties.
3102⠀
3103In any case, after putting a teaspoon of honey into your hot drink, all antioxidants won’t disappear at once - they simply won’t have time to do it, because your mug is not an autoclave.
3104⠀
3105Perhaps no one has studied the effect of HMF on human health and its long-term consequences. But taking into account our daily use of a wide variety of products with HMF throughout our lives, this organic compound should not cause serious concerns.
3106⠀
3107When heated, other organic compounds can form in honey that are harmful to the body (or affect it ambiguously), however, they do not make heated honey noticeably more toxic than almost any other food that you fry or bake.
3108⠀
3109Moreover, due to the acidic environment (pH 3.5), HMF also appears in raw honey.
3110⠀
3111In the continuation of the article, we"ll analyze why citric acid is sometimes added to the dough for a honey cake and what can be used instead of honey.
3112⠀
3113part 3
3114 
3115While I was reparing materials for the articles on my site, I found out that one very interesting topic was not fully disclosed.
3116 
3117Almost a year and a half ago I started writing an article about honey, and in the first two parts I gave a detailed answer to such a tricky question as to whether it's possible to heat honey and whether it becomes harmful after that.
3118 
3119If you are not familiar with this information or want to refresh it in your memory, follow the hashtag #marusya_about_honey.
3120 
3121Today I'd like to analyze another important question:
3122 
3123🌿 What are honey substitutes and how to use them in the recipe?
3124 
3125Allergy prevents some people from consuming this product, that's why we need to find a suitable replacement.
3126 
3127❗️ATTENTION❗️
3128 
3129The allergens present in honey are subjected to heat treatment during baking, after which most of them break down, and in most cases, cease to cause an allergic reaction!
3130 
3131However, if you want to be 100% sure or cook to order for an allergic client, there are 3 main replacement options:
3132🔸 glucose-fructose or glucose syrup
3133🔸 trimoline
3134🔸 artificial honey
3135 
3136You can read more about these ingredients here #marusya_about_sugar .
3137 
3138💎 Approximate ratios of replacing honey with glucose / glucose-fructose syrup / artificial honey — 1:1; trimoline — 1:0.75
3139 
3140Also, sometimes honey is replaced with maple or rice syrup, molasses, agave nectar. As you can see, there are lots of options.
3141 
3142Many people often ask another question:
3143 
3144🌿Can I replace sugar with honey?
3145 
3146When replacing sugar with honey, remember that it's sweeter than sugar. In addition, sugar and honey have different densities, so a 1:1 substitution will make your baked goods not only very sweet, but also sticky, heavy, and overly moist.
3147 
3148💎 Ratios of replacing sugar with honey
3149 
3150For every 100 g sugar indicated in the recipe, use 75 g honey.
3151part 4
3152 
3153#marusya_about_honey
3154 
3155Guys, thank you for being so active under the previous post — I see that the topic of honey is really interesting for you, and therefore I decided not to put off writing the continuation.
3156 
3157🌿 Things to remember when replacing sugar with honey:
3158 
3159🔸 increase baking time as the dough becomes more “heavy” and needs to be baked longer
3160🔸 lower the temperature by 15 °C, as honey gives the dough a darker color
3161🔸 reduce the amount of liquid in the recipe; reduce or increase the amount of flour
3162 
3163Summing up, it’s definitely possible to replace sugar with honey. However, be sure to stick to the right ratio and, if necessary, make adjustments based on the thickness, consistency and sweetness of the honey used.
3164 
3165🌿Why add citric acid or lemon juice to honey dough?
3166 
3167Adding citric acid is an optional step in the preparation of honey dough and it's just to be on the safe side in case baking soda fails to react properly. If it hasn't activated, you may face several issues:
3168 
3169🔸 smell of baking soda during baking
3170🔸 bitter aftertaste in cakes
3171🔸 cake with frosting / berry or fruit filling can be streaked blue — frosting or filling contains acid that reacts with not activated baking soda in the cake layers
3172🔸 flat, thin crepe-like cake layers
3173 
3174I advise you not to neglect this step, since there is always a possibility that the soda won’t react properly for the following reasons:
3175 
3176🔸 different composition of natural honey (more/less acidic, more or less acid in the composition of different types of honey)
3177🔸 different composition of alkalized cocoa (more/less acidic, more or less acid in the composition of cocoa with varying degrees of alkalization)
3178🔸 wrong temperature that doesn’t ensure a proper reaction between baking soda and acid
3179 
3180💎 Ratio of adding lemon juice to honey dough
3181 
31821-1.5 g per every 100 g of dough
3183 
3184Part 2
3185 
3186#marusya_about_ganache
3187 
3188One of the last posts of 2022 was the dedicated to ganache theory. I am very pleased that you liked that post, so here’s the continuation😉
3189 
3190As we have already discussed, ganache is a mixture of chocolate and liquid. Now let's talk in detail about the chocolate used to make ganaches.
3191 
3192The following types of chocolate can be used for making ganaches:
3193▪️white
3194▪️milk
3195▪️dark
3196▪️bitter
3197 
3198Chocolate can be used in any form:
3199▪️chocolate bars (tablets)
3200▪️chocolate blocks (chunks)
3201▪️chocolate callets (chips)
3202▪️chocolate drops
3203 
3204I usually work with callets, as they help to speed up the melting of chocolate when in contact with hot liquid.
3205 
3206📎 the main requirement is that callets should be stored in sealed packaging, protected from moisture — if water gets into chocolate, its shelf life decreases and the quality of the product deteriorates
3207 
3208Impact factors
3209 
3210⚫️TEXTURE
3211 
3212▪️ thanks to the cocoa butter, ganache becomes fluid when heated and sets when cooled (due to the crystallization of cocoa butter molecules)
3213▪️the lower the cocoa butter content and the higher the sugar content are, the softer the consistency of the ganache is
3214 
3215💎for making ganache the chocolate should contain at least 18% cocoa butter and 40-70% cocoa products (these figures are usually indicated by manufacturers on the packaging)
3216 
3217📎By cocoa products I mean the content of cocoa butter and cocoa mass (cocoa powder).
3218 
3219⚫️ TASTE
3220 
3221▪️ characteristic flavor of chocolate:
3222 
3223⚪️white – sweet milky
3224🟤milk – vanilla-caramel
3225⚫️dark – rich chocolate
3226 
3227▪️ characteristic chocolate bitterness: it reinforces in ganache due to the increased content of cocoa products (70% or more) and is leveled when the content of cocoa products is around 50% (medium).
3228part 3
3229 
3230#marusya_about_ganache
3231 
3232Apart from chocolate, ganache must contain liquid. But here is where it gets fun: you can use different liquids, e.g:
3233 
3234⚪️ Fresh or pasteurized cream
3235 
3236The fat content of cream should be 35-40%. Thanks to minimal heat treatment, the finished ganache will have a rich creamy taste, but at the same time ‒ a reduced shelf life.
3237 
3238⚪️ Sterilized or UHT cream
3239 
3240The fat content is 35-37%. These types of cream are exposed to high temperatures for a short period of time. This process extends the product's shelf life making these creams chefs’ favorites when working with ganaches.
3241 
3242⚪️ Milk
3243 
3244Is used to replace some or all of the cream in ganache. Unsweetened condensed milk (also called evaporated or concentrated milk) is often used to reduce the amount of water added, which negatively affects the shelf life of the ganache.
3245 
3246🟣 Fruit or berry puree
3247 
3248Part of the cream can be replaced with puree. Most often, the ratio of puree and cream is 1:1. It is added to give ganache a fruity flavor and aroma, as well as interesting vivid colors.
3249 
3250💎DOSAGE
3251 
3252▫️milk: maximum 40%
3253▫️cream: 20-50%
3254▫️puree: maximum 50%
3255 
3256How the liquid added affects the ganache?
3257 
3258🟣 TEXTURE
3259◽️ gives the ganache a silky, melting texture during tasting (provided the fat content is at least 35%)
3260◽️ in some cases, e.g. when preparing whipped ganache, cream is a necessary element for increasing the volume, as well as trapping and stabilizing air bubbles in the finished ganache
3261 
3262🟣 MOISTURE CONTENT
3263◽️ liquid with a water content of about 60% dissolves sugar and makes the chocolate less dense
3264 
3265🟣 TASTE
3266▫️ due to the fat molecules present in the composition, it has aroma-retaining properties and is a carrier of taste to our receptors
3267Part 4
3268 
3269So, did you miss this series of posts?
3270 
3271Today we continue talking about ganaches and as usual, I’m ready to share with you a concentrate of the best information from top pastry chefs and chocolatiers, seen through the prism of my experience and critical thinking.
3272 
3273Check out the beginning here #marusya_about_ganache
3274 
3275We’ve already analyzed the chocolate and liquid in the composition of ganache in detail, now let's move on to possible additives.
3276 
3277➕ Butter
3278🔺fat content — 82% and more — the more fat contained in butter, the longer the shelf life of the finished ganache will be
3279🔺 the butter is added when the ganache is at 32–34ºС
3280 
3281💎 dose — 5–15%
3282 
3283📌recommended dose of fats (butter + cocoa butter) — 25–40% — too much fats will destabilize the ganache (the emulsion will break), while insufficient amount of fats will result in a ganache that’s too thick
3284 
3285When added to a ganache, the butter influences its:
3286 
3287🔴 TEXTURE
3288 
3289▪️when the butter is heated, the consistency of the ganache becomes runnier, and when it cools, the ganache becomes thicker
3290▪️makes the ganache smooth and creamy, adds it a glossy shine, as well as a delicate, melt-in-the mouth texture (provided the butter is added at the end of cooking, at 30-35ºС, without prior melting).
3291 
3292🔴 TASTE
3293 
3294▪️ has aroma-retaining properties and is a carrier of taste to our receptors due to fat molecules in the composition
3295▪️ reduces the bitterness of chocolate with a high content of cocoa products, e.g., >70%.
3296 
3297🔴SHELF LIFE
3298 
3299▪️ adding butter to cream before boiling it prolongs the shelf life of the ganache, and also contributes to the even distribution of fat molecules in the mixture.
3300Part 5
3301 
3302#marusya_about_ganache
3303 
3304In the previous part we discussed all the nuances of adding butter to a ganache. But apart from it, the following ingredients can be added to the ganache, namely:
3305 
3306➕ Sugar
3307🔺sugar in ganaches can be represented by: sucrose (=sugar), invert sugar or glucose syrup (high in dextrose)
3308💎 dose — at least 25% of the total weight
3309 
3310When added to a ganache, sugar influences its:
3311 
3312🔴 TASTE
3313 
3314▪️makes the ganache sweeter
3315 
3316🔴 TEXTURE
3317 
3318▪️makes it softer and more elastic
3319▪️increases the viscosity of the ganache, making it more stable due to its hygroscopic properties
3320 
3321🔴 SHELF LIFE
3322 
3323▪️ prolongs shelf life — sugar helps to “bind” water present in cream, making it unavailable for the microorganisms (molds) to multiply.
3324 
3325➕ Sorbitol or food supplement E420
3326It is an odorless white crystalline powder with a pleasant taste. It is two times less sweet than regular sugar and is very hygroscopic (absorbs and retains moisture).
3327💎 dose — 1.5–2.5% of the total weight
3328 
3329The way sorbitol affects the ganache is similar to sugar, however, E420 is used primarily as a stabilizer, and not as a sweetener.
3330 
3331➕ Aromatic additives
3332🔺 can be represented by various extracts, essential oils, alcohol, as well as flavored cream or milk
3333🔺 all additives that are sensitive to microflora (cocoa powder, spices, etc.) should be boiled for several minutes along with the cream

3348Today I will share with you a couple more useful tips and tricks.
3349 
3350🤔 Why has your ganache split?
3351 
33521. It doesn't contain enough liquid
33532. Poor dispersion of fats in water
33543. Indoor temperature is too high
3355 
3356🆘 How to fix split ganache?
3357 
33581. Add heavy cream/milk/puree
33592. Process the mixture with a hand blender
33603. Process the mixture with a hand blender and allow it to cool
3361 
3362↘️↙️
3363⠀⬇️
3364 
3365And here comes another interesting nuance:
3366 
3367📍 Ganache should be processed with a hand blender while it is hot to avoid air bubbles getting into the mixture! This is due to the fact that:
3368 
3369🔺 air is a favorable environment for microorganisms
3370🔺 fats that are in contact with air eventually turn rancid
3371 
3372♻️ How and how long can ganache be stored?
3373 
3374✔️ Before storing and cooling the ganache, it should be covered with cling film that comes in contact with the surface of the ganache. This way, you will protect your ganache from air, microorganisms and evaporation of aromatic substances.
3375 
3376✔️ Ganaches can be stored in the fridge at 4ºС for no more than 2 days. You can freeze them only in finished products.
3377WHY DO YOU NEED TO SIFT THE FLOUR?
3378 
3379Almost every pastry recipe calls for sifted flour. Have you ever thought about why you should sift it?
3380 
3381Let's start with what we have actually moved away from for a long time, thanks to modern production technologies.
3382 
3383In the past, it was always necessary to sift the flour in order to:
3384 
3385▪️ get rid of weevils and other bugs
3386⚠️Nowadays contaminated foods should be disposed immediately.⚠️
3387▪️ get rid of the smallest pieces of stone for grinding grain — quern. The saddle quern consisted of a flat stone bed and a rounded stone to be operated manually against it; later, millstones developed from those hand-operated querns
3388▪️ get rid of debris — threads from packing bags, cereals from a neighboring box, pieces of paper or plastic bags, etc.
3389 
3390Nowadays, here are a few times you should sift your flour for baking:
3391 
3392▪️ to eliminate and prevent lumps — when your flour has been sitting around for a while, seems to be tightly packed, or has been stored at high humidity, lumps form even in high-quality flour.
3393▪️ to get a homogeneous, uniform base for the dough — sifted flour mixes better with other ingredients, and the dough behaves more predictably
3394▪️ to get much lighter and airier flour — it’s especially important when baking sponges
3395▪️ to guarantee you get the right amount when the recipe uses cups instead of grams as a unit of measure (American recipes) — sifted flour will have a larger volume than not sifted
3396 
3397💡 Lifehack from Marusya
3398 
3399If the flour you are using is fairly fresh and does not contain any lumps, in most cases you can skip sifting, and make your flour airier simply by mixing it with a whisk.
3400HONEY CAKE LAYERS: FACTORS THAT AFFECT THE INTENSITY OF HONEY TASTE AND AROMA
3401 
3402Honey cake layers can have a different intensity of honey taste and aroma: from delicate, subtle honey notes to strong, pronounced ones. What does it depend on?
3403 
3404The very first and seemingly obvious answer that comes to mind is the amount of honey added. But in fact, this is far from the primary factor.
3405 
34061️⃣ The intensity of the honey taste and aroma in the cake layers depends primarily on the heat treatment the batter undergoes before baking — the more you heat the honey mixture, the more pronounced taste will have the cake layers:
3407 
3408🔸subtle honey taste and aroma can be obtained by bringing the mixture for the honey dough to 60–70 °C over a water bath or in a saucepan
3409🔸 moderately pronounced taste is obtained by bringing the honey or the honey mixture to a boil (100–116 °C)
3410🔸 rich, pronounced honey taste and aroma of the cake layers is achieved by boiling the honey mixture or by using the dough made with burnt honey (> 116 °C)
3411 
34122️⃣ Secondly, the intensity of the honey taste and aroma of the cake layers depends on the type of honey. If you want to get cakes with a light, delicate honey taste, opt for light types of honey, for example, acacia or linden. Buckwheat honey and other dark types should be preferred if you like cakes with a rich, pronounced honey taste.
3413 
34143️⃣ The amount of honey added to the dough makes less difference to the intensity of honey taste and aroma of cake layers compared to the type of honey and its heat treatment. Therefore, do not be surprised that the amount of honey in honey recipes can vary from 60 to 450 g, depending on the recipe.
3415 
3416P.S. The photos show the Pear–Gorgonzola–Pine Nut honey cake, which drives fans of the sweet fragrant pear and noble blue cheese crazy.
3417 
3418Do you think the dough for this honey cake is cooked over a water bath or is brought to a boil? Those who vote for the water bath, type "1" in the comments, for boiling — type "2".
3419BROWN VS. WHITE EGGS — IS THERE A DIFFERENCE?
3420 
3421#marusya_theory_tips
3422 
3423Guys, you probably noticed that in some countries in supermarkets the prices for brown and white eggs differ? Is there any reason for that? Or is it just a marketing ploy?
3424 
3425Let's figure it out. But first things first.
3426 
3427Is there really a difference between brown and white eggs?
3428 
3429✔️ Yes — in color
3430 
3431Eggs come in many colors, depending on the breed of the hen. Chickens have different pigment genes that determine the color of the egg shell. Generally speaking, white shell eggs come from hens with white feathers and white earlobes, while brown shell eggs are produced by hens with brown feathers and red earlobes.
3432📍some chickens have a unique pigment gene and lay multi-colored eggs — light blue, green and even pink
3433 
3434✖️ No — in terms of nutritional value and taste
3435 
3436The color of the eggshell is not indicative of the best quality of the egg. In fact, there is no distinguishable difference between brown and white eggs when it comes to taste and nutritional value.
3437📍The nutritional value and palatability of an egg depend solely on the diet and environment of the chicken. The food eaten by the hen can directly affect how an egg tastes, which means that pasture-raised hens usually lay eggs that have a richer taste and higher concentration of useful macro- and microelements
3438 
3439❓ Is the higher price for brown eggs a marketing ploy?
3440 
3441✖️No — hens with brown feathers tend to be larger than hens with white feathers, meaning they consume more feed. Therefore, brown eggs need to be sold at a higher price to make up for the extra costs
3442 
3443✔️ Yes — if you have faced an unscrupulous dealer speculating on their more "natural" appearance

34818 TYPES OF BUTTER AND HOW TO USE THEM
3482 
3483start by tag #marusya_about_butter
3484 
3485Well, the start of a new week! Wish you all the best and a productive Monday!
3486 
3487Today I’ve prepared a continuation about the types of butter. Learn, save it to your bookmarks and don’t forget to leave your ❤️ and any comment under the post — I will be very pleased!
3488 
3489So, we have already talked about sweet cream, salted, compound and ghee butter. There are 4 more types left.
3490 
3491🧈Cultured butter
3492 
3493This is the secret ingredient in the best bakery products! This type of butter is obtained by adding pure cultures of lactic acid bacteria to cream (hence the European name cultured butter) and fermentation of the mixture for at least 24 hours. In the process of the fermentation we obtain a thick product with a pronounced cultured milk aroma that enhances the flavor of dishes with the addition of butter, such as pancakes, pies or mashed potatoes.
3494 
3495🧈European-style butter
3496 
3497European butter can boast about its high fat content of 82% to 90%. The high fat content with longer-lasting whipping and live cultures gives European butter a bright yellow color and rich flavor that is instantly transferred to bakery products.
3498 
3499🧈Whipped butter
3500 
3501This fresh look at a traditional butter involves adding air to it to create an easy-to-butter ingredient. Using a hand or planetary mixer make creamy whipped butter that keeps its smooth texture even after cooling. You can also add milk for a softer consistency. Put whipped butter on a crispy piece of baguette and enjoy a restaurant-worthy snack!
3502 
3503🧈Brown butter (beurre noisette)
3504 
3505Brown butter is an ultra-luxury ingredient that adds a nice nutty flavor to everything from bread and cake to Brussels sprouts. Heating of diced sweet cream butter until the milk solids begin to turn brown gives a toasted and delicious butter that is incredibly fragrant in both liquid and solid form.

3548HOW TO RECALCULATE THE CHOCOLATE PERCENTAGE🍫
3549 
3550#marusya_about_chocolate
3551 
3552More than enough times, I've been faced with complaints about broken ganache and tearful pleas for help.
3553 
3554I will help.
3555 
3556In 99% of cases, beginners simply replace the amount of chocolate specified in the recipe with exactly the same amount of chocolate with a different percentage of cocoa products.
3557 
3558DON’T DO THIS
3559 
3560Is it possible basically to replace one chocolate with another?
3561 
3562✔️ Yes, if you correctly calculate the required amount of chocolate with a cocoa butter content that is different from that specifed in the recipe
3563 
3564Let's say the recipe says 400 g of dark chocolate 54% (chocolate A). You only have 64% chocolate (Chocolate B) available.
3565 
3566How to recalculate?
3567 
3568The 54% marking reflects the total content of cocoa products (cocoa butter and cocoa liquor). For instance, 100 g of 54% chocolate may contain 45 g of sugar (45%), 19 g of cocoa liquor (19%) and 36 g of cocoa butter (36%).
3569📌 see the cocoa butter content on the package or clarify with the manufacturer
3570 
3571Enter the factor value
3572 
3573factor = cocoa butter content/100
3574 
3575The recipe uses 400 g of 54% chocolate (which is 19% cocoa liquor and 36% cocoa butter), and you want to replace it with 64% chocolate, which contains 41% cocoa butter
3576 
3577factor A (chocolate 54%) = 36% (cocoa butter)/100 = 0.36
3578factor B (chocolate 64%) = 41% (cocoa butter)/100 = 0.41
3579 
3580We have calculated the factor value for both types of chocolate, now we need to calculate how much of the available chocolate 64% we will need, according to the formula:
3581 
3582x = (weight of chocolate A * factor A) / factor B
3583 
3584Substitute the values:
3585 
3586x = (400 g * 0.36) / 0.41 = 351 g
3587 
3588That is, you can replace 400 g of 54% chocolate with 351 g of 64% chocolate that you have available.
35898 TYPES OF BUTTER AND HOW TO USE THEM
3590 
3591#marusya_about_butter
3592 
3593Any butter is made from cream. But depending on the origin of the cream, the composition of the butter and ways of preparation, there are several varieties that ensure different tastes of meals and serve for different purposes:
3594 
3595🧈Sweet Cream Butter (Unsalted Butter)
3596 
3597It is the most popular variety due to its versatility: it can be used both for frying vegetables, meat, and for making desserts and pastries. It is made only from cream of animal origin.
3598 
3599🧈Salted Butter
3600 
3601Unsalted butter is all cream, while the salted variety has some salt added. Salt acts as a preservative, so this variety of butter lasts longer than the unsalted one. Most often, it is used for spreading on bread, topping veggies and pasta.
3602 
3603🧈Ghee butter
3604 
3605Gee is nothing more than the pure butterfat! It is cooked over low heat to remove the water molecules until the milk solids brown lightly, creating a slightly nutty, caramelized aroma. Ghee butter is shelf-stable and has a high smoke point. It is most commonly used for sautéing vegetables, meat, but it’s also used in baked goods thanks to its rich flavor.
3606 
3607🧈 Compound butter
3608 
3609This is a special variety of butter that has been softened to a pliable state, and then has herbs, spices, and/or sweeteners (fruit or berry purees) added to it. Usually chilled and then sliced, compound butter can add herby richness to a steak, enhance the flavor of a baked good, or add creaminess to a pasta sauce.
3610ALT. PASTRY CHEF'S NOTES

3616 
3617So, today we continue to analyze the topic of salt.
3618 
3619Earlier, we talked about why add salt to desserts — #marusya_about_salt. Having understood that salt performs many important functions, from enhancing flavor to extending the shelf life of desserts, the question arises: how much salt to add?
3620 
3621First of all, the amount of salt added to a dessert will depend on the variety of salt you are using.
3622 
3623VARIETIES OF SALT
3624 
3625There are 4 types of food salt:
3626 
3627▫️table salt
3628▫️rock salt
3629▫️sea salt
3630▫️lake salt
3631 
3632They differ from each other in the methods of harvesting, chemical composition — and, accordingly, taste, as well as varying degrees of salinity, depending on the content of sodium chloride in the composition.
3633 
3634▪️TABLE SALT
3635 
3636The most commonly used variety of salt. Most table salt is mined, then put into water, purified of other trace minerals and re-dehydrated (evaporated). The content of NaCl is no less than 97% on a dry matter basis.
3637 
3638There are different grades of table salt:
3639 
3640🔸 second
3641🔸 first
3642🔸 top
3643🔸 "Extra"
3644 
3645The main feature of "Extra" grade is that it is fine-grained (almost like a powder) and often comes in "iodized" form which means it includes a small amount of potassium iodide or sodium iodide. The addition of iodine does not change the taste of the salt itself, but when heated above 100 °C, iodized salt decomposes and gives a dish a metallic aftertaste.
3646 
3647"Extra" grade salt has a distinctive taste. Such salt is convenient to use in food production: its high degree of salinity and low cost reduce the cost of purchasing and storing the product.
3648part 2
3649 
3650Judging by the number of comments under yesterday's post, we should definitely create a cupcake recipe book! I'm glad you're so excited about this idea. The team and I will make sure to bring it to life. As soon as the recipe book is available on the site, I’ll notify you in a post and story.
3651 
3652Today, as promised, I will continue talking about food salt.
3653 
3654#marusya_about_salt
3655 
3656▪️ROCK SALT
3657 
3658It is considered natural: it is obtained by mining, then ground and it’s ready to be used. It has a less intense taste than that of table salt.
3659 
3660▪️SEA SALT
3661 
3662A type of salt of natural (marine) origin, extracted from seawater through the use of a vacuum evaporation process after which it is sun and wind dried and then distilled into an edible, solid form. The purity of sea salt depends on the composition and purity of the sea water from which it is harvested.
3663 
3664Sea salt not only enhances the taste of foods, but can also add different flavors depending on the additives in its composition. It is much less salty than table or rock salt, so when cooking, you need much more of it to make the dish salty.
3665 
3666▫️Fleur de Sel ("flower of salt" in French)
3667The most expensive among sea salts. It has an exceptional taste and is able to give dishes a special delicacy and sophistication. This salt has snowflake-like crystals which add a crunchy texture to dishes.
3668 
3669▪️LAKE SALT
3670 
3671It is extracted from the bottom of salt lakes, where it settles naturally. Then it undergoes purification processes, after which the salt crystals are converted into food salt. Lake salt has a low sodium chloride content, so it is considered less salty.
3672 
3673Classification of salt on the basis of salinity levels from higher to lower:
3674 
36751. Table salt "Extra" grade
36762. Table salt of the first, second and top grades
36773. Rock salt
36784. Sea salt
36795. Lake salt
3680 
3681In all my recipes I use rock salt. Therefore, if you use any other type of salt, make sure to reduce / increase its amount based on the degree of salinity.
3682part 3 and last
3683 
3684Today we will figure out how much salt should be added to desserts and pastries, and we will also talk about what kind of salt is used in confectionery.
3685 
3686How much salt to add? This usually depends on:
3687 
3688◾️ variety of salt
3689We have already figured out that, first and foremost, the amount of salt added to dessert depends on the type of salt you use, since each of them has a different degree of salinity. You can read about it in detail by following the hashtag #marusya_about_salt
3690 
3691◾️ goal you want to achieve
3692If you want just to enhance the taste of the dessert, then you will need less salt than to eliminate excessive sweetness, for example.
3693 
3694◾️ your taste preferences
3695Depending on taste buds, a dessert with the same amount of salt will be moderately salty for some, and slightly over-salted for others.
3696 
3697✅ Basic ratios of salt to flour and sugar:
36981/4 tsp. salt per cup (130 g) flour
36991/4 tsp. salt per cup (180 g) sugar
3700 
3701If the dessert you are making contains both flour and sugar, then first use salt in ratio only to the ingredient that weighs more, check the result, and then add more salt if necessary.
3702 
3703⚠️Which variety of salt to use in pastry?
3704When choosing the type of salt to add, think of the goal you want to achieve:
3705 
3706◾️ if you need salt to decorate your dessert, then opt for Fleur de Sel — it has snowflake-like crystals and ennobles the dessert with its crispy texture. Other varieties of sea salt that have a crystalline structure are also suitable for decoration.
3707 
3708◾️ to enhance the taste of the dessert, I advise you to use rock or sea salt. They dissolve well in the dough and don’t add bitterness, which is typical of iodized and table salt.
3709 
3710◾️ if you use salt as a bitterness neutralizer, why add an extra bitter taste along with table salt?
