6182CARAMELIZATION AND THE MAILLARD REACTION - IS IT THAT EASY TO MAKE CARAMEL?
6183Part 1
6184⠀
6185I feel like writing about something profound. Especially taking into account a recent conversation with one of my curious students. She was wondering why in one caramel recipe the cream is added first, and then comes the butter, while in the other it's vice versa – first we introduce the butter, and then pour in the cream.
6186⠀
6187So, what's the difference?
6188⠀
6189There are three large groups of reactions resulting in browned food:
61901. Fermentation
61912. Caramelization
61923. The Maillard reaction
6193⠀
6194We’re only interested in the last two groups, since they take place not only in the process of making caramel, but also in many other culinary processes.
6195⠀
6196Caramelization is a complex chemical process that takes place when sugar is being heated.
6197⠀
6198Sugar molecules subjected to high temperatures break down into characteristic aromatic fragments, which in turn form new compounds – polymers. They produce a familiar caramel taste and amber color.
6199⠀
6200The more complex the sugar molecule, the higher its caramelization point.
6201⠀
6202For example, for fructose (a monosaccharide) it’s 110°C / 230°F, while for sucrose (regular white sugar, a disaccharide) – 160°C / 320°F.
6203⠀
6204The caramelization point of isomalt is 170°C / 338°F, which allows it to be heated for a long time without changing its color to brown.
6205⠀
6206There are two ways to caramelize sugar:
6207⠀
6208🌿Dry method
6209- only sugar is used
6210- requires some practice
6211- no risk of sugar crystallization during the caramelization process.
6212⠀
6213🌿Wet method
6214- water and sugar are used (1: 2)
6215- gradual heating of the syrup and evaporation of water facilitates the process of making caramel
6216- if you make a mistake in cooking, there is a risk of sugar crystallization.
6217⠀
6218The caramelization issue seems to have been sorted out. No tricks: sugar + high temperature = caramelization.
6219⠀
6220In the continuation of the article I’ll tell you about the Maillard reaction and explain the difference between adding the cream and the butter to the caramel first.
6221Part 2.
6222 
6223Russian version 👉🏻@ma_rusya_manko
6224 
6225The Maillard reaction is a chemical reaction between an amino acid and a reducing sugar, usually requiring the addition of heat.
6226⠀
6227Excellent example is the crust of roast meat or baked bread which has the typical smell, color and taste of cooked food. These changes are caused by melanoidins - compounds generated in the late stages of the reaction.
6228⠀
6229It is named after a French physician and chemist Louis Camille Maillard, who was one of the first to study the reaction in the 1910s. There are thousands of melanoidins and other compounds, and about two dozen of them are actively used as flavor additives in food chemistry.
6230⠀
6231So, the Maillard reaction = [protein (free amino acids) + sugar (monosugars)] + temperature (not necessarily high)
6232⠀
6233Acidity plays a crucial role in this process. The higher the acidity is, the slower the Maillard reaction occurs.
6234⠀
6235It means it will never occur when grilling kebabs in a sour marinade. It is for this reason that tomato paste is put into demi-glace sauce only at the last stages, and at the first come bones, meat and vegetables.
6236⠀
6237Moreover, you should pour the lime juice into the pho broth at the end, but a couple of tablespoons of palm sugar are to be put immediately. So, in no case should you marinate the steak in something sour - you will end up with just marinated meat, not a steak.
6238⠀
6239Now let's look at the caramel.⠀
6240The thing is that when we first add 80-100 g of butter (~ 10°C/ 50°F) to 200 g of dry caramel (~ 180 °C / 356 °F), the temperature of the mass quickly drops to ~ 105-110°C / 221-230°F. Then, by pouring in the cream, we will reduce the temperature of the caramel further.
6241⠀
6242On the contrary, when the hot cream is gradually poured in, the temperature decreases moderately and stays in the range of 140-160°C / 284-320°F longer.
6243⠀
6244Therefore, in addition to caramelization of sugars, the Maillard reaction also takes place - amino acids in the cream react with sugars, which enhances the aroma and provides a darker shade of caramel.
6245ECTIN, AGAR-AGAR AND GELATIN. WHAT’S THE DIFFERENCE?
6246 
6247Russian version 👉🏻@ma_rusya_manko
6248⠀
6249Agar-agar is obtained from seaweed, pectin - from apples and citrus fruits, sometimes from sunflower, pumpkin and sugar beet.
6250⠀
6251This is how they differ from gelatin, a product of animal origin that is made from many different sources of collagen, mostly from cattle bones and hides, pigskin and fish.
6252⠀
6253To understand which is better - agar-agar, pectin or gelatin, let’s see how each of them works.
6254⠀
6255Pectin gives products a soft, delicate, slightly viscous consistency. It has no color, but it can add a light sourness.
6256⠀
6257There are many types of pectin: some need a lot of sugar, that’s why they are used for making very sweet masses (jam, confiture, pate de fruit), others work in tandem with calcium that allows us to use them in dairy products. There are pectins of fast and slow setting, thermostable, thermo-reversible, etc.
6258⠀
6259Agar-agar does not have its own taste and calorie content that’s why it is highly valued by confectioners. The use of agar allows you to get a stable thick mass, which will hold the given shape even in the heat. This is very important in making jelly, pastila and pate de fruit.
6260⠀
6261Agar-agar is thermo-reversible - it can be reheated and then cooled again without loss of quality. This is convenient when one portion of the thickener wasn’t enough and you need to add thickness to the mass.
6262⠀
6263What is the difference between agar-agar and gelatin?
6264⠀
6265📌 The gelling ability of agar-agar is much better than that of the gelatin, that’s why in the preparation process you need 3 times less of it.
6266⠀
6267📌 Agar-agar sets faster (even at room temperature), while gelatin needs a fridge to set, and all the products made with it melt at 40 °C / 104 °F.
6268⠀
6269📌 Agar-agar can be dissolved in juices, but gelatin does not like fruit acids, it should be dissolved in water only.
6270⠀
6271If you need a more detailed analysis of agar-agar and gelatin, their strengths and properties, let me know in the comments 👌🏼
6272⠀ SOUR CREAM AND CREAM – LET’S HACK THE ISSUE!
6273⠀ ⠀
6274I suggest starting with the whipping cream.
6275⠀
6276Thanks to the separation, it is possible to produce the cream with different fat content:
6277 
6278📍Whipping cream 30-35%
6279📍Light cream 20%
6280📍Half and Half 10-12%
6281⠀
6282Half and half is a mix of ½ whole milk and ½ cream, typically added to coffee, tea or hot cocoa. This dairy product is good for cooking the creamy baked meat, for example. It also can be used for making the savoury custard for a quiche, you may cook porridge with it or add to pureed soups.
6283⠀
6284Light cream is quite thick, but even if it whips, it will quickly collapse.
6285⠀
6286Therefore, the light cream is not suitable for making the cream. But it is perfect for making sauces, it also can be added to cream soups. In most cases it is added to coffee and used for baking.
6287⠀
6288Whipping cream is the thickest and most nutritious type of cream.
6289⠀
6290This cream is used for making mousses, creams, pastries, as well as ice cream. This is the cream pastry chef constantly works with.
6291⠀
6292Cream with a fat content of at least 33% is considered ideal for whipping. I personally prefer whipping cream with a fat content of 36% - any cream comes out much more stable than using 30-33% whipping cream.
6293⠀
6294An important point. Mostly, we work with either very hot whipping cream ( ~ 90 °C / 194 °F - caramel, ganache) or very cold one (4 °C / 39 °F - whipped creams, mousses).
6295⠀
6296And what about homemade cream? Can we work with it? I can’t categorically say "no", but I do not advise working with it either. Why?
6297⠀
6298I'll tell you in the continuation of a series of posts about sour cream and cream 😏⠀
6299This time we will talk about sour cream. The name of this product is familiar to everyone speaking Slavic languages. In European countries, sour cream became widespread only in the XX century.
6300⠀
6301Among the Slavic peoples, its name sounds almost the same: сметана, смятана, smotana, smantana, smietana. The Germans call this product schmand or schmetten.
6302⠀
6303Residents of foreign countries often call sour cream "Russian cream". In many countries there is a product very similar to sour cream. It is called crème fraîche. We will talk about the difference between these products and their interchangeability later.
6304⠀
6305Sour cream is obtained by fermenting regular milk or cream with certain kinds of lactic acid bacteria. This can be done both at home and in the industry.
6306⠀
6307Depending on the manufacturing method, sour cream is divided into two types:
6308 
6309📌 produced by households (farm-fresh) obtained by natural fermentation of milk
6310⠀
6311📌 produced by industrial production, made from whole milk or pasteurized cream with the addition of pure streptococcal cultures.
6312⠀
6313The first type has a thicker consistency and a taste similar to butter. The advantages of the second are the possibility of producing sour cream with different fat content and a longer shelf life.
6314⠀
6315Based on the percentage of fat, sour cream produced by industrial production can be:⠀
6316📌 Light (10-14%)
6317📌 Regular (15-19%)
6318📌 Classic (20-34%)
6319📌 High-fat (35-48%)
6320⠀
6321In the pastry industry sour cream of 30-40% fat is used most often since it can be whipped. Sour cream is used to make sweet unleavened dough, tea-cakes, muffins, cookies, pies, sometimes sponges and, of course, for making creams.
6322⠀⠀
6323Let's talk about the quality of sour cream and the possibility of substituting it with other products.
6324⠀
6325Sour cream is a delicate white dairy product with a moderately thick, homogeneous consistency and glossy surface.
6326⠀
6327Having the smell, typical for pasteurized dairy products, good-quality sour cream has a pronounced, but pure taste of fermented milk with slight sourness.
6328⠀
6329Too strong acidic taste or sour smell, the presence of grains in sour cream are the indicators of violations in the production technology or storage conditions of the product.
6330⠀
6331Both farm fresh and industrial sour cream should not contain anything except milk (cream) and ferments, otherwise this product cannot be called sour cream.
6332⠀
6333In this regard, it is worth paying attention to the ingredients and name of fermented milk products. The absence of the word combination "Sour cream" on the packaging, as a rule, means the presence of vegetable fats among the ingredients of such products.
6334⠀
6335⠀
6336What can be used to substitute sour cream in frosting?
6337⠀
6338I can suggest you several options to choose from, and the first one, preferable, is crème fraîche.
6339⠀
6340Crème fraîche (lit. from French "fresh cream") is a dairy product with the fat content of not more than 30% (usually 15—18 or 28%).
6341⠀
6342It is made by fermenting pasteurized cream from cow's milk. Crème fraîche has a pleasant, slightly sour taste, and without additives it is indistinguishable from sour cream either in taste or in the production technology.
6343⠀
6344In England, Spain, Italy, Denmark, the Benelux countries, crème fraîche and sour creame often stand on the shelves of shops nearby and even have the same manufacturers. Moreover, both names are often present on the same package.
6345Can we work with homemade cream? I can’t categorically say “no”, but I don’t recommend working with it either. Why? Let's figure it out.
6346⠀
6347Firstly, in addition to different fat content, the cream can also be divided into categories according to the method of heat treatment:
6348⠀
6349📌Raw – natural.
6350📌Pasteurized - heated up to 80 ± 2 °C / 176 °F for 20 sec and then cooled to 4 ± 2 °C / 39 °F.
6351📌Sterilized - heated up to 137 ± 2 °C / 279 °F for 4 sec and then cooled to 20 °C / 68 °F.
6352⠀
6353I think it is obvious to everyone that homemade cream is sold completely fresh, not subjected to any heat treatment, that significantly reduces its shelf life.
6354⠀
6355The shelf life of homemade cream is from 12 hours to several days, depending on its fat content.
6356⠀
6357That’s why a cake made with fresh homemade cream should be eaten not later than the next day after assembling.
6358⠀
6359Secondly, most often the fat content of homemade cream which can be purchased on the market is about 50-60%. It’s not very convenient, but you can work with it if you dilute the cream with whole milk.
6360⠀
6361But this is where another problem arises. Since you do not know the exact fat content of your homemade cream, you might make a mistake when diluting it with milk - make it too runny or leave it too fatty.
6362⠀
6363Let’s draw the conclusions. It is definitely possible to use fresh homemade cream for making frosting for a cake, but only for a cake that is meant for your own home consumption. I do not recommend working with it, especially if you bake cakes to order.
6364⠀ 📍Why should we chill the whipping cream before whipping?
6365⠀
6366The answer is super simple – by doing this we let the fats crystallize. Otherwise, the whipping cream will quickly collapse or won’t whip up at all. In addition, it is recommended to cool also all the tools involved in the work with it, i.e. a whisk and a bowl.
6367⠀
6368I haven’t done this for a long time, and I don’t advise you to do it. Why?
6369⠀
6370Nowadays almost any whipping cream in its contents contains various food additives, namely stabilizers, thickeners and often preservatives. Thanks to these additives, it whips well and keeps its shape perfectly without any problems.
6371⠀
6372📍How to check the quality of whipping cream at home?
6373⠀
6374To do this, simply bring the whipping cream to a boil and look at its consistency. If your hot whipping cream becomes runny like milk, everything is fine, you can work with it.
6375⠀
6376If, on the contrary, it has become even thicker, almost like sour cream, I doubt that fats of animal origin predominate in its contents.
6377⠀
6378📍Why does the packaged whipping cream with a normal shelf life sometimes happens to be as thick as homemade fat sour cream?
6379⠀
6380Well, it happens sometimes. Dishonest suppliers are to blame for this, because they violated the storage conditions of the product and exposed the whipping cream to temperature extremes to which the latter is extremely sensitive.
6381⠀
6382If this happens to you, do not hesitate to ask the supplier to replace such product with the one it’s possible to work with.
6383Today I suggest talking about the difference between sour cream and crème fraîche.
6384⠀
6385So, nevertheless, crème fraiche and sour cream are far from the same thing. If we talk about sour cream that can be purchased abroad, then it will most likely be a kind of “sour cream product”, and not real sour cream.
6386⠀
6387Since we will be looking for a substitute for sour cream in non-CIS countries, I’d like to compare the crème fraîche and the “sour cream product”, which is usually sold abroad in packages labelled “sour cream”.
6388⠀
6389📌 The fat content of the “sour cream product” may vary from 10 to 24%. "Sour cream" with the fat content of 15% is the easiest to find. Find the one with a higher fat content may be problematic.
6390⠀
6391📌 The fat content of crème fraîche is 15-35%. The product with a fat content of 30% is the most common.
6392⠀
6393📌 “Sour cream” may typically include ingredients such as gelatine, pectin, renin, modified starch, sodium citrate, and plant enzymes. They are used to stabilize and thicken the product.
6394⠀
6395📌 Crème fraîche does not contain added thickeners and stabilizers. It is much thicker and has a richer and creamier flavor compared to “sour cream”.
6396⠀⠀
6397📌 “Sour cream” has a pronounced sour taste.
6398⠀
6399📌 Crème fraîche is a dairy product with a pH of approximately 4.5. It is less acidic than sour cream.
6400⠀
6401In recipes where crème fraîche and sour cream are not the main ingredient, they can be used interchangeably.
6402⠀
6403Since crème fraîche contains more fat and less protein, it may be used in soups and sauces - it will melt easily and won't curl, unlike sour cream.
6404⠀
6405So, what is better to use for making frosting for a honey sponge cake - "sour cream" or crème fraiche? It will depend on what kind of frosting you want to make and the result you want to get.
6406Today let’s figure out what's better to use for frosting — low-fat sour cream or crème fraîche.
6407⠀
6408To make frosting for a honey cake, you can use either crème fraîche or sour cream with a low fat content that contains thickeners.
6409⠀
6410In the first case, you will get a very thick, stable frosting, and it will be a pleasure to assemble a cake with it. It's perfect for tall cakes with complex fillings.
6411⠀
6412Main disadvantage - crème fraîche will not give a pronounced sourness typical of sour cream.
6413⠀
6414Using sour cream with a fat content of less than 25-30%, you will get an unstable frosting that won’t keep its shape at all.
6415⠀
6416Thickeners in the contents won’t add thickness to the frosting — the sour cream containing thickeners can be quite stable itself, but if you add sugar to it and start whipping, the whole mixture turns into a runny substance that is difficult to whip and thicken.
6417⠀
6418Unstable frosting can be used to assemble a classic honey sponge cake in a cake ring, but the result will be corresponding — too soaked cake and no distinct stripes in the cut.
6419⠀
6420What can be done? First of all, try to drain the sour cream using a strainer and a doubled or tripled piece of cheesecloth. If the amount of thickeners and stabilizers in its contents is not enormous, then over the night of draining your 15-20% sour cream will turn into 30-35%, while losing up to 40% of its weight.
6421⠀
6422Optionally, you may replace a part (up to 50%) of the sour cream in the frosting with 33-36% heavy cream or mascarpone, for example.
6423⠀
6424Ideally, if you use 15-20% sour cream for the frosting, first drain it in the fridge overnight, and then whip with heavy cream.
6425⠀⠀
6426If you've read to the end and liked the article, please let me know in the comments. This is extremely important to me, as every comment you leave inspires me to write more!
6427⠀⠀
6428P.S. In the photos you can see a classic honey sponge cake with 30% sour cream. Then, in the carousel, there are photos of the same honey sponge cake with frosting made with farm-fresh sour cream with high fat content. Do you see the difference?
6429 
6430Today we will find out what else can be used instead of sour cream in recipes, except for crème fraîche.
6431 
6432So, apart from crème fraîche, sour cream can also be replaced with
6433📌Greek yogurt with high fat content
6434📌Mascarpone with cream
6435 
6436Now let's have a closer look at pros and cons of these options.
6437 
6438My students who live abroad often write me that instead of sour cream they use Greek yogurt and everything turns out just fine - the frosting is light and the cake is delicious.
6439 
6440Possibly.
6441 
6442It is possible if you have access to high fat yogurt abroad, which can be used in making a good frosting.
6443 
6444My first and last experience with Greek yogurt frosting ended in disaster.
6445 
6446The frosting turned out to be good - stable and tasty. I assembled the honey sponge cake without any problems. But what I saw the next morning just shocked me.
6447 
6448There was a puddle of whey under the cake. Quite a real puddle, which I diligently wiped out with a paper towel, already perfectly aware that the experiment had failed.
6449 
6450In short, about 2 tablespoons of liquid had been appearing under the cake every 2 hours. You may imagine the cut and taste of that honey sponge cake - it was as if the cake had been dipped in a container filled with water for several hours.
6451 
6452The result, in principle, is logical - there are only 10-12% of fat in yogurt. It is almost the same as using 10% sour cream.
6453 
6454From my experience, even 2-3 tablespoons of yogurt added to the frosting with cream and cream cheese will significantly reduce the stability of the latter.
6455 
6456Thus, I only accept the use of “fat” yogurt for frosting if it has been drained well at least overnight, then mixed with heavy cream and / or mascarpone. But this version of frosting is not suitable for all types of honey layers.
6457 
6458Have you tried using yogurt in frosting instead of sour cream? Share your experience, please.
6459 
6460As for the recipes in which sour cream is used for making the dough, in most cases you can safely replace it with Greek yogurt and kefir or buttermilk.
6461 
6462A PERFECT CHEESECAKE. MYTHS VS REALITY
6463⠀
6464«...Why should the ingredients for a cheesecake be of a room temperature? Does it mean that if I take ingredients right from the fridge, nothing will work?
6465⠀
6466Which tool should be used to properly combine the ingredients for the cheese mass - a spoon, a spatula or a whisk? You say any of them except from a whisk, but why?
6467⠀
6468Great, then what happens if you take cold ingredients and give them a good whisk?🤔
6469⠀
6470Nothing terrible?! No way!! But what about cracks, lumps and other horrifying consequences that everyone is talking about? .. "
6471⠀
6472⠀
6473Surely, everyone who has ever tried to bake a cheesecake has asked similar questions. Especially after reading various tips.
6474⠀
6475Let's try to deal with them and understand the real reasons why such superstitions appear.
6476⠀
6477⠀
6478In fact, the temperature of the ingredients does not affect the taste, texture or appearance of a cheesecake at all. Only the temperature of the cream cheese can be of minor importance, as the cream cheese becomes soft at room temperature. That’s why the cheese mass with room temperature cream cheese will be much easier to mix until smooth, avoiding lumps.
6479⠀
6480If you forgot to take the cream cheese out of the fridge in advance, don’t worry. If desired, it can be slightly heated up over a water bath or in a microwave oven, or even used cold.
6481⠀
6482If you are a lucky owner of a Kenwood Cooking Chef Induction Mixer, you can mix the cold cheese mass turning on the heating at 40 °C / 104 °F. It is very convenient.
6483⠀
6484The cheese mass for a cheesecake has to be mixed until smooth, but not whipped until fluffy. Therefore, work at low speed using the paddle attachment.
6485⠀
6486So what will happen if you whisk cold ingredients together?
6487⠀
6488In fact, absolutely nothing to worry about. The mass will be actively saturated with air, that’s why its volume might increase significantly.
6489⠀
6490Still, you may face some difficulties such as:
6491📌 the whole cheese mass won’t fit into the shortbread shell
6492📌after baking, the excess air will come out of the cheesecake and a dent will form in the center. Its depth directly depends on how much you whipped the cheese mass.
6493HOW TO MAKE A PERFECT CHEESECAKE
6494 Personally, I prefer the hot infusion, since it is much more effective and does not require such a long preparation.
6495⠀
6496The hot method suggests heating the whipping cream / milk with flavoring up to 90 °C / 194 °F over low heat. Then you should cover the mixture with a lid and let it infuse for at least 2-3 hours. For an intense flavor, you may repeat the process one more time.
6497⠀
6498If you use flowers, leaves, coffee grains, tonka beans or tea as a flavoring, it’s a good idea to process the whipping cream together with the flavoring with a blender before heating.
6499⠀
6500But in this case you should increase the amount of whipping cream / milk by 30-40%, and after infusion simply strain the mass through a strainer or a piece of cheesecloth.
6501⠀
6502ℹOnly a metal cake pan with a removable bottom is good for baking cheesecake.
6503⠀
6504At first glance, this is a correct statement, because in this case unmolding the cheesecake can be done with your eyes shut. In fact, cheesecakes can be baked equally well in cake pans with removable bottom and in baking rings.
6505⠀
6506Just when dealing with baking rings, beginners will immediately face two seemingly difficult tasks:
6507⠀
6508☑how to shape and then transfer a cheesecake in a baking ring without a bottom, because after baking the dessert is unstable and requires careful treatment.
6509⠀
6510There are several solutions to this problem.
6511⠀
6512In order to easily transfer the cheesecake to the oven and then to the fridge, you can place the baking ring on the bottom of the old split cake mold of a slightly larger diameter, covered with a sheet of parchment paper.
6513⠀
6514I use another method - I wrap the bottom of the baking ring in several layers of foil and put it on a thick cake base.
6515⠀
6516You can, of course, put the ring directly on the baking sheet, provided that its surface is perfectly flat, but in this case you will not be able to use it until the cheesecake is completely cooled. To be continued.
6517 
6518⠀
6519☑how to unmold a seemingly glued cheesecake from a baking ring without destroying its ideal shape.
6520⠀
6521Firstly, remember to apply a thin layer of oil (I prefer coconut oil) on the baking ring and unmold the cheesecake out only after 6 hours of cooling.
6522⠀
6523Secondly, if you leave a cheesecake at room temperature for 15-20 minutes, the oil will slightly melt and you will only need to carefully lift the baking ring up. The cheesecake will remain on the cake base.
6524⠀
6525✅Useful tips:
6526⠀
6527✔To speed up the process of unmolding the cheese, you may heat the baking ring up with a heat gun or a hair dryer.
6528⠀
6529✔Preferably, the cheesecake should not be higher than 6 cm (without décor).
6530⠀
6531✔Try to shape the sides of the shortbread shell so that the cheese mass is only slightly below the level of the sides, otherwise it may detach slightly after baking.
6532⠀
6533ℹCheesecake must be baked at low temperatures (not higher than 120 °C / 248 °F) with a water bath.
6534⠀
6535Let me remind you that a water bath is a baking sheet full of boiling water installed on the lowest level of the oven.
6536⠀
6537Please, pay your attention to the fact that you should not pour water into the baking sheet with the cheesecake, otherwise the water will get into the mold and your cheesecake will get wet.
6538In fact, the baking mode for a cheesecake depends on the characteristics of your oven. Cheesecakes can be baked at either 90-100 °C / 194-212 °F or 150-160 °C / 302-320 °F, with or without a water bath.
6539 
6540If you bake your cheesecake at a very low temperature (90-100 °C / 194-212 °F), then you probably won't need a water bath. But if your oven is so powerful that even at 90 °C / 194 °F the cheesecake rises and its top burns - put a water bath - it lowers the heat in the oven.
6541 
6542I used to bake cheesecakes with a water bath and strictly at a temperature not more than 120 °C / 248 °F. It would take my cheesecakes at least 3-4 hours to bake, and for a long time I could not understand what I was doing wrong.
6543 
6544Then a brilliant idea came across my mind and I bought a thermometer for the oven. It turned out that my 120 °C / 248 °F + a water bath = 80-90 °C / 176-194 °F of real temperature. Now, when baking a cheesecake with a water bath, I set the temperature to at least 150 °C / 302 °F and my thermometer shows exactly 120 °C / 248 °F. The cheesecake is ready in 1.5 hours!
6545 
6546ℹYou can bake cheesecakes using absolutely any oven mode.
6547 
6548It all depends on the situation, and, again, on the characteristics of your oven. If the top heating element heats too much, then turn on only the bottom one. In 80% of cases, conventional heating is the best option. If you need to bake several trays at the same time, use the convection mode.
6549 
6550✅Useful tip:
6551If you have difficulties with the baking mode and the uniformity of baking, you can cover the top of the cheesecake with foil. This will keep the light color of the cheesecake and prevent it from being burnt.
6552 
6553⠀
6554✅Add the flavouring directly to the cheese mass.
6555⠀
6556🔹️What could it be?
6557⠀
6558✔berry, fruit purees
6559✔whole berries and fruit pieces
6560✔caramel, dulce de leche
6561✔chocolate, ganache, Nutella
6562✔nuts and nut paste
6563✔instant coffee, matcha tea
6564✔liqueurs, cognac and other alcoholic beverages.
6565⠀
6566You need to work very carefully, since each of these flavourings has a different water and sugar content. The most important advice I want to give is that berry or fruit purees should be pre-thickened with cornstarch.
6567⠀
6568Add the starch to the puree and constantly stirring bring it to a boil - you should get a thick mass. It is also possible to go without pre-cooking, but then the texture of the cheesecake will be a little softer.
6569⠀
6570If you add alcohol, then reduce the cream / milk in the recipe by the same amount, if you do not want the cheesecake to be very tender.
6571⠀
6572✅Infuse cream or milk.
6573⠀
6574🔹️You may infuse it with:
6575⠀
6576✔cinnamon stick, vanilla pod, saffron, whole star anise, cardamom, or any other spice
6577✔lavender and violets flowers, rose petals
6578✔leaves of mint, lemon balm, basil, thyme
6579✔coffee beans
6580✔tonka beans
6581✔lemon, orange or lime zest
6582✔green, black, milk or any other tea
6583✔blue cheeses and more.
6584⠀
6585There are two ways of infusion- cold and hot one.
6586 
6587As for the cold method, it is enough to add flavoring to the cream / milk and leave it overnight, or even better for a day, in the fridge.
6588⠀
6589PERFECT BORDERS
6590 
6591Perhaps the most difficult thing in the process of baking cheesecakes is to achieve perfectly beautiful borders, both externally and in the cut.
6592⠀
6593For sure, the even borders take your cheesecake from the category of an ordinary homemade pastry to the rank of a culinary masterpiece, don’t they?
6594⠀
6595By the way, how are you doing with the borders? Are you satisfied with your result?
6596⠀
6597At the very beginning, when I baked my first cheese cakes, making a crust was the most lengthy and painful moment of the whole process.
6598⠀
6599I tried almost all the tools! Glasses, mugs, spoons, dough scrapers - I've tested everything! I used to spend at least 40-50 minutes for one cheese cake. There was even a period when I awaited this moment with horror and panic.
6600⠀
6601But hard work and experience overcame everything. Six months later, the perfect base was ready in 15 minutes using a spoon as an improvised tool.
6602⠀
6603Now I form an ideal crust in 4-5 minutes, sometimes with only my bare hands staying absolutely calm.
6604⠀
6605What's the secret, you ask? Is it just a vast experience?
6606⠀
6607Not really. The secret of a quick and perfect base for a cheesecake is a good recipe and the right technique. Well, experience, of course, will come in handy 😉
6608⠀
6609⠀
6610A few tips and tricks for you:
6611⠀
6612📌 reconstituted shortbread should shape well and keep its shape when squeezed, while remaining crumbly
6613⠀
6614📌 both butter and coconut oil can be used to change the structure the shortbread
6615⠀
6616📍in the latter case, the base will be more crumbly and crunchy, and it will also be much more easier to unmold it
6617⠀
6618📌forming the base of the cheesecake, try to distribute the crumbs along the bottom and sides in an even layer
6619⠀
6620📌 pay attention to the fact that the thickness of the crust from bottom to top should be the same
6621⠀
6622📌 also make sure that a thick layer of dough does not form between the bottom and the borders
6623⠀
6624📍 in order to avoid the latter, first completely form the borders, and only then proceed to the bottom
6625HOW TO MAKE A PERFECT CHEESECAKE
6626 
6627I am glad that you find the tips on making the perfect cheesecake useful, so I will be happy to continue. If you like this idea, leave + in the comments!
6628⠀
6629⠀
6630ℹIs it necessary to use room temperature ingredients to make the cheese filling?
6631⠀
6632No, it won’t affect the taste, the texture or the look of the pastry in any way. In fact, only the temperature of the cream cheese matters, because when the cream cheese is at room temperature, it is much softer and then it’s easier to mix the mass until smooth, avoiding lumps.
6633⠀
6634Useful tip:
6635⠀
6636✅If you forgot to get the cream cheese out of the fridge beforehand, you may slightly heat it up over a water bath or in the microwave oven.
6637⠀
6638⠀
6639ℹDo not whip the mass for a long time, otherwise there might be cracks on the surface of the cheesecake due to the excess of air in the mass. Work at low speed.
6640⠀
6641In fact, it is enough to understand that the cheesecake should be mixed until smooth, and not whipped until fluffy.
6642⠀
6643As for the cracks that appear due to air bubbles, I'm sure this is more of a myth. There were several cases when I used cold cream cheese and overwhipped the cheese mass trying to get rid of the lumps. There were bubbles, but no cracks.
6644⠀
6645The reason for the formation of cracks on the surface of the cheesecake is actually very simple - the baking temperature is too high, but I will write about it in detail later in the article.
6646⠀
6647✅Useful tip:
6648⠀
6649It is better to introduce the eggs into the cheese filling one at a time, each time mixing the mass until smooth.
6650⠀ WATER BATH AND CHEESECAKE
6651⠀
6652One of the questions that I receive quite often from people who bought the cheesecake recipe concerns the water bath: what’s that? Where should I pour the water?
6653⠀
6654I'm wondering, without reading the post, what are your ideas? Write them in the comments section, please! I will read your answers with pleasure.
6655⠀
6656Done? Great! Now let's figure out how the cheesecake should be baked.
6657⠀
6658In fact, you may bake a cheesecake both over a water bath and in a water bath, or even without using water at all. So, what is the water bath in cooking in general and how is it better to bake a cheesecake - over or in the water bath?
6659⠀
6660💦 A water bath is a method of cooking, in which the liquid is heated without direct contact of the container with fire. This method is used when the required temperature of the mass that is being cooked should not exceed 100 °C / 212 °F.
6661⠀
6662💦 Cooking over a water bath suggests pouring the water into the lower bowl or container, and placing the product, which will be cooked over a water bath, in the upper one. In this case, the height of the upper bowl should be smaller in order to leave room for boiling water. Moreover, the water in the larger bowl should not boil too much. This construction is usually used for cooking on the stove.
6663⠀
6664💦 Cooking in a water bath suggests placing a small bowl directly into a larger container, into which the hot water is poured. The level of water should reach the middle of the inner bowl. This construction is often used for baking in the oven.
6665⠀
6666When baking over the water bath, the cheesecake is placed on a baking sheet / wire rack at the top of the oven, while the second baking sheet or any other container filled with hot water is placed at the bottom.
6667⠀
6668When baking in the water bath, the cheesecake pan is placed directly into a large container filled with hot water.
6669⠀
6670I do not recommend baking the cheesecake in the water bath, as there is a high chance of water getting into the mold resulting in a wet base of the cheesecake!
6671⠀
6672HOW TO MAKE A PERFECT CHEESECAKE
6673⠀
6674I suggest paying closer attention to this popular American dessert and trying to figure out together which rules for its preparation should be considered as indisputable facts, and which can be neglected.
6675⠀
6676Also, in this article, I will share some tips that will help you to make not only incredibly delicious, but also gorgeous-looking cheesecake.
6677⠀
6678Let’s bust the myths! And I’ll start with making the shortbread crust.
6679⠀
6680ℹThe crust can be made using the ready-made or home-made shortbread cookies, you won't notice much difference.
6681⠀
6682Although I completely agree with the first part of the statement, the second seems quite controversial. If you want to make a really delicious cheesecake, do not be too lazy to bake home-made sables with almond or hazelnut flour.
6683⠀
6684Useful tips:
6685⠀
6686✅While forming the base of the cheesecake, try to distribute the crust along the bottom and sides of the springform pan in an even layer. Mind the thickness of the sides and the bottom, it should be the same.
6687⠀
6688✅Make sure that the edges are not thick. To avoid this common problem, do the sides first, and only then proceed to the bottom.
6689⠀
6690✅It is very convenient to use a flat bottom mug (not the one with the concave bottom) with sides at right angle for forming the cheesecake base. Also, a tablespoon might be quite helpful.
6691⠀
6692✅If after baking the sides of the shortbread crust have slid down, replace the butter with a thicker one and try to use less of it.
6693⠀
6694✅It is much easier and faster not to bake the formed shortbread crust, but simply put it in the freezer for 30-60 minutes.
6695⠀
6696✅If you use the coconut oil instead of butter for the reconstituted shortbread crust, the base will come out crispier and crumblier, and it will be easier to unmold it.
